Now, we connect this to the practical initialization
\mathbf{A}^{(0)}
required by Theorem
1
. The condition required by the theorem is
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}\leq C\alpha\beta^{-1}\phi^{2}\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})
, which is an
\mathcal{O}(1)
bound that does not depend on
T
.
From (
C.7
), we know that the target solution
\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{opt}}
lies within an
\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/2})
neighborhood of the true parameter
\mathbf{A}^{*}
. In high-dimensional statistics, it is standard that a well-behaved procedure, such as the ALS algorithm described in Section
C.5
, when run for a moderate number of steps, is sufficient to produce an estimate
\mathbf{A}^{(0)}
that falls within the required
\mathcal{O}(1)
error basin.
Therefore, we can conclude that our practical initialization
\mathbf{A}^{(0)}
satisfies the condition
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}\leq C\alpha\beta^{-1}\phi^{2}\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})
required by Theorem
1
, provided
T
is sufficiently large.

We now invoke the computational convergence result from Theorem
1
:

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1})^{J}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}+C\eta_{0}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}.

(C.8)

From our analysis, the initial error
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}
is a constant of
\mathcal{O}(1)
, while the statistical error term
\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}
is of order
\mathcal{O}(1/T)
. The bound thus becomes:

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1})^{J}\cdot\mathcal{O}(1)+C\eta_{0}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}.

(C.9)

Since
\rho=(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1})
is a constant less than 1, the computational error term
\mathcal{O}(1)\cdot\rho^{J}
decays to zero geometrically. Therefore, for a sufficiently large constant number of iterations
J\geq J_{0}
, the computational error is absorbed by the statistical error term. The total error is dominated by the statistical error:

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}\leq C^{\prime}\eta_{0}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}.

(C.10)

Furthermore, substituting the high-probability bound for
\xi
from Lemma
C.2
:

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}\leq C_{\text{final}}\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df_{NLR}}}{T}.

(C.11)

Finally, from Lemma
B.2
, we have

\|\mathbf{A}^{(J)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\|\mathbf{B}_{2}^{(J)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1}^{(J)}-\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df_{NLR}}}{T}.

(C.12)

This concludes the proof.
∎

C.2
Proof of Corollary
1

From the proof of Theorem
2
, we have established that for
J\geq J_{0}
, the overall estimation error
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}
is bounded by the statistical error:

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}\leq C\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df_{NLR}}}{T}.

(C.13)

Bounds for
\beta_{\textup{A}}
and
\beta_{\textup{N}}

Since the constraint
\|\mathbf{B}_{1}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
, with high probability, the optimal scaling factors
c_{1},c_{2}
are close to 1.
By the definition of
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}
and Lemma
B.2
, we have:

\begin{split}&(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A}}-\beta_{\textup{A}}^{*})^{2}\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)},\\
&(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{N}}-\beta_{\textup{N}}^{*})^{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}.\end{split}

(C.14)

Substituting
\|\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\phi^{2}
and the bound for
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}
from (
C.13
), we get:

(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A}}-\beta_{\textup{A}}^{*})^{2}\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\phi^{-2}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}\lesssim\phi^{-2}\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df_{NLR}}}{T}.

(C.15)

Moreover,
\mathrm{df_{NLR}}=2+r+2Dr
, and
\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=N
, thus

(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A}}-\beta_{\textup{A}}^{*})^{2}\lesssim\phi^{-2}\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{Dr}{NT}.

(C.16)

The bound for
(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{N}}-\beta_{\textup{N}}^{*})^{2}
follows identically.

Bounds for Projectors
\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{U}}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}}

To bound the projectors, we first need to bound the error of the
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
matrix itself. From Lemma
B.2
, we have a bound relating the optimally scaled matrix error to
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}
:

\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2}-\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\phi^{-2}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}.

(C.17)

Now, we apply Lemma
C.8
. This lemma directly bounds the squared Frobenius norm of the difference in projectors:

\|\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{U}}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}^{*}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{2\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2}-\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{\delta^{2}},

(C.18)

where
\delta=\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})
.
Substituting our bound for
\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2}-\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
:

\|\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{U}}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}^{*}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}\left(\phi^{-2}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}\right)=\phi^{-2}\delta^{-2}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}.

(C.19)

Finally, substituting the bound for
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(J)}
from (
C.13
) and
\mathrm{df_{NLR}}=2+r+2Dr
, we obtain:

\|\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{U}}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}^{*}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\phi^{-2}\sigma^{-2}_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{Dr}{T}.

(C.20)

The bound for the row-space projector
\|\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{V}^{*}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
is derived using the identical relations mentioned in Lemma
C.8
. This completes the proof.

C.3
Verification of RSC and RSS Conditions

The MAR(1) model is
\mathbf{Y}_{t}=\mathbf{B}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\top}+\mathbf{E}_{t}
. Let
\mathbf{y}_{t}=\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t})
and
\mathbf{e}_{t}=\text{vec}(\mathbf{E}_{t})
. Then, it is equivalent to the following VAR process

\mathbf{y}_{t}=(\mathbf{B}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1})\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\mathbf{e}_{t}.

(C.21)

The least squares loss function is

\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{B}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\left\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(C.22)

It is easy to check that, for any
N\times N
matrices
\mathbf{B}_{1},\mathbf{B}_{1}^{*}
,
D\times D
matrices
\mathbf{B}_{2},\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}
,
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{B}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1}
, and
\mathbf{A}^{*}=\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1}^{*}
,

\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})-\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})-\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*})\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(C.23)

Firstly we prove the RSC and RSS conditions, formally stated in the following lemma.

Lemma C.1
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, if
T\gtrsim(4Dr+4)M_{1}^{2}
, then with probability at least
1-C\exp\{-c(4Dr+4)\}
, for all matrices
\mathbf{A}
of the form
\mathbf{A}=(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{\top})\otimes(\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N})
and the truth
\mathbf{A}^{*}=(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})\otimes(\beta_{\textup{A}}^{*}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}^{*}\mathbf{W}_{N})
, we have

\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*})\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

(C.24)

where
\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}=\sigma_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/(2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A}))
,
\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}=3\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/(2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A}))
and

M_{1}=\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\mu_{\max}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)/\left(\sigma_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\mu_{\min}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)
.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

Denote
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}
,
R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}):=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\mathbf{Z}=(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{T})
. Note that

R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})=\frac{1}{T}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{T})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}\|\text{vec}(\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{Z})\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}\|(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p})\text{vec}(\mathbf{\Delta})\|_{2}^{2}.

(C.25)

Let
\boldsymbol{\delta}=\text{vec}(\mathbf{\Delta}),\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}}=\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}/T
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}=\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}})
, then we have

R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}/T\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p})\boldsymbol{\delta}=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p})\boldsymbol{\delta}.

(C.26)

Note that
R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\geq\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\sup\limits|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
, we will derive a lower bound for
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
and an upper bound for
\sup\limits|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
to complete the proof of RSC.

By spectral measure, we have
\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Gamma})\geq\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{e})/\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})
. Then for
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
, by (
C.26
) and properties of Frobenius norm, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})&=\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p})\boldsymbol{\delta}\right]\\
&=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}(\mathbf{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p})\boldsymbol{\delta}\\
&\geq\left\|\boldsymbol{\delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\sigma_{\text{min}}(\mathbf{\Gamma})\\
&\geq\left\|\boldsymbol{\delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{e})/\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A}).\end{split}

(C.27)

Now suppose that
\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1.
We define the set of unit-norm differences of Kronecker products as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)=\{\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{A}_{1}-\mathbf{A}_{2}:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{i}=(\mathbf{U}_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\top})\otimes(\beta_{\textup{A}i}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}i}\mathbf{W}_{N}),

(C.28)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{i},\mathbf{V}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r},\ \beta_{\textup{A}i},\beta_{\textup{N}i}\in\mathbb{R},\ \left\|\mathbf{M}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1,i=1,2\}.

Then
\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)
. For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, consider an
\varepsilon
-net of
\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)
with respect to the Frobenius norm, denoted by
\overline{\mathcal{S}}(r,D;N)
. Then for any
\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)
, there exists
\overline{\mathbf{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}(r,D;N)
, such that
\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}-\overline{\mathbf{\Delta}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon
. By Lemma
C.6
, we have

|\overline{\mathcal{S}}(r,D;N)|\leq\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{4Dr+4}.

(C.29)

In addition, By Proposition 2.4 in
Basu and Michailidis, 2015
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that for any single vector
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}D^{2}}
with
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}=1
, any
\eta>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(|\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}[(\widehat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}-\mathbf{\Gamma})\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p}]\boldsymbol{v}|>2\pi\mathcal{M}(f_{\mathbf{Z}})\eta\right)\leq 2\exp[-cT\min(\eta,\eta^{2})],

(C.30)

where
\mathcal{M}(f_{\mathbf{Z}})\leq\sigma_{\max}(\Sigma_{e})/2\pi\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})
.

Now, we focus on
\sup|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
. Note that
R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}[(\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}}-\mathbf{\Gamma})\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p}]\boldsymbol{\delta}.
With the covering net, we can transfer the region of supremum from unit sphere to
\varepsilon
-net.

\begin{split}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|=&\left|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}[(\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}}-\mathbf{\Gamma})\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p}]\boldsymbol{\delta}\right|\\
:=&\left|\text{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}_{t})\text{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right|\\
\leq&\left|\text{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}_{t})\text{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right|+2\left|\text{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}_{t})(\text{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\text{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}))\right|\\
&+\left|(\text{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})^{\top}-\text{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})^{\top})\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}_{t})(\text{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\text{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}))\right|\\
\leq&\max_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}}|R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})|+(2\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2})\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|.\end{split}

(C.31)

When
\varepsilon\leq 1
we have

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\leq(1-3\varepsilon)^{-1}\max_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}}|R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})|.

(C.32)

Then by (
C.29
) and (
C.30
) , we yields that for some
\tau
¿ 1,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq 2\pi\tau\mathcal{M}(f_{\mathbf{Z}})\eta\right)\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}}|R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})|\geq 2\pi\tau\mathcal{M}(f_{\mathbf{Z}})\eta(1-3\varepsilon)\right)\\
\leq&\sum_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}\left(|R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})|\geq 2\pi\tau\mathcal{M}(f_{\mathbf{Z}})\eta(1-3\varepsilon)\right)\\
\leq&2\exp\left[-cT\min(\eta,\eta^{2})+(4Dr+4)\log(C/\varepsilon)\right].\end{split}

(C.33)

Due to the arbitrariness of
\eta
and
c
, we still use the original letters here.

Then we set
\eta=[\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{e}})]/[4\pi\tau\mathcal{M}(f_{\mathbf{Z}})\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})]<1
, and then we obtain that for

T\gtrsim(4Dr+4)[(\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{e}})\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A}))/(\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{e}})\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A}))]^{2}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\frac{\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{e})}{2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})}\right)\leq C\exp\{-c(4Dr+4)\}.

(C.34)

Hence, when
T\gtrsim(4Dr+4)[(\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{e}})\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A}))/(\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{e}})\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A}))]^{2}
, we have that with probability at least

1-C\exp\{-c(4Dr+4)\}
,

R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\geq\frac{\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{e})}{2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})}.

(C.35)

Then
\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}:=\sigma_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/(2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A}))
.

As for RSS part, similarly we have
R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\leq\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})+\sup\limits|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
and
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\sigma_{\text{max}}(\mathbf{\Gamma})\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\sigma_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{e})/\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})
by spectral measure. Since the event

\left\{\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\leq\frac{\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{e})}{2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})}\right\}

(C.36)

implies

\left\{\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\leq\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{e})}{2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})}\right\},

(C.37)

we have when the high-probability event occurs,

R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\leq\frac{3\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{e})}{2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})}.

(C.38)

Thus
\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}:=3\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A}).

∎

C.4
Property of Deviation Bound

In this section, we establish a high-probability upper bound for the deviation bound
\xi
. This term is a critical component of the statistical analysis, quantifying the statistical error inherent in the estimation problem. The following lemma provides a non-asymptotic bound, showing that this deviation is controlled by the model’s degrees of freedom
(2+r+2Dr)
and diminishes at the rate of
1/\sqrt{T}
as the sample size increases.

Lemma C.2
.

Define

\xi:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r},\\
\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1,\\
\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}}\in\mathbb{R},\\
\|\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\ \mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\otimes(\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N})\right\rangle.

(C.39)

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, if
T\gtrsim(4Dr+4)M_{1}^{2}
, then with probability as least
1-C\exp\left(-CDr\right)
,

\xi\lesssim M_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}}{T}},

(C.40)

where
M_{2}=\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/\mu_{\min}^{1/2}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
,
\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}=2+r+2Dr
and
M_{1}
is defined in Lemma
C.1
.

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{1}(N)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}:\mathbf{Q}=\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N},\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}}\in\mathbb{R},\textrm{ and }\|\mathbf{Q}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1\right\},~\text{and}

(C.41)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{2}(r,D)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}:\mathbf{Q}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{\top},\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r},\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{U})=\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{V})=r,\mathrm{and}\|\mathbf{Q}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1\right\}.

Furthermore, define

\mathcal{V}(r,D;N):=\left\{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{1}:\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}(r,D),\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}(N)\right\}.

(C.42)

Obviously,
\mathcal{V}(r,D;N)
defined in (
C.42
) is equivalent as definition in (
B.13
). Let
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(N)
and
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(r,D)
be the
\varepsilon/2
-covering net of
\mathcal{Q}_{1}(N)
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(r,D)
, respectively. By Lemma
C.5
and
C.4
,
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(N)\subset\mathcal{Q}_{1}(N)
,
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(r,D)\subset\mathcal{Q}_{2}(r,D)
and

|\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(N)|\leq\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{2},~~|\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(r,D)|\leq\left(\frac{18}{\varepsilon}\right)^{r+2Dr}.

(C.43)

Then
\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N):=\left\{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\otimes\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(r,D),\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(N)\right\}
is an
\varepsilon
-covering net of
\mathcal{V}(r,D;N)
and

|\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)|\leq\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{2+r+2Dr}.

(C.44)

To see this, for any
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{V}(r,D;N)
, choose
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(r,D)
and
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(N)
such that
\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon/2
and
\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon/2
. Then

\left\|\mathbf{A}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\otimes\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|(\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2})\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\otimes(\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon.

(C.45)

Meanwhile, it is clear that
\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})=(\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top})/T
, where
\mathbf{e}_{t}
and
\mathbf{y}_{t}
are defined at the begining of this section. Then

\xi(r,D;N)=\sup_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{V}(r,D;N)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{A}\right\rangle.

(C.46)

Next, we establishing an upper bound for
\xi
.

For every
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{V}(r,D;N)
, let
\overline{\mathbf{A}}=\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\otimes\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)
be its covering matrix. By splitting SVD (Lemma
C.3
), we know that
\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}
can be decomposed as
\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}
are both rank-
r
and
\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\rangle=0
. For
\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}
, there exist
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}=\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1}
. Then
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}/\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}(N)
and
\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon/2
. And with Cauchy’s inequality and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, we know that
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}/2\varepsilon.
Therefore, since
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,i}/\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,i}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}(r,D),i=1,2,
we have

\begin{split}&\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{A}\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle\\
&+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},(\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2})\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\otimes(\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{1})\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\otimes\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{1}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{1}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
\leq&\max_{\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle+\xi\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)\\
\leq&\max_{\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle+\sqrt{2}\varepsilon\xi.\end{split}

(C.47)

Hence,

\xi\leq(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)^{-1}\max_{\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle.

(C.48)

For any
ND\times ND
real matrix
\mathbf{M}
, we define
R_{T}(\mathbf{M}):=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
and
S_{T}(\mathbf{M}):=T^{-1}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{t},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\right\rangle
. Then we note that

\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle=S_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}}).

(C.49)

By the proof of Lemma S5 in
Wang et al., 2024
, for any
z_{1}
,
z_{2}\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{S_{T}(\mathbf{M})\geq z_{1}\right\}\cap\left\{R_{T}(\mathbf{M})\leq z_{2}\right\}\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{z_{1}^{2}T}{2\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)z_{2}}\right).

(C.50)

Then for any
x\geq 0
, with the probabilistic upper bound of
R_{T}(\mathbf{M})
(
C.38
), we have when
T\gtrsim(4Dr+4)M_{1}^{2}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\xi\geq x\right)\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle\geq(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)x\right)\\
\leq&\sum_{\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\geq(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)x\right)\\
\leq&\sum_{\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{S_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\geq(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)x\right\}\bigcap\left\{R_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\leq\frac{3\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)}{2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})}\right\}\right)\\
&+\sum_{\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)}\mathbb{P}\left(R_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\geq\frac{3\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)}{2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})}\right)\\
\leq&|\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r,D;N)|\left(\exp\left\{-\frac{(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)^{2}Tx^{2}\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})}{3\sigma_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)}\right\}+C\exp\{-c(2+r+2Dr)\}\right).\end{split}

(C.51)

Here we take
\varepsilon=0.1
and
x=C_{1}^{1/2}\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\mu_{\min}^{-1/2}(\mathcal{A})\sqrt{(2+r+2Dr)/T}
, where
C_{1}\gg C
, then

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\xi\geq C_{1}^{1/2}\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\mu_{\min}^{-1/2}(\mathcal{A})\sqrt{(2+r+2Dr)/T}\right)\\
\leq&\left(\frac{C}{0.1}\right)^{2+r+2Dr}\left(\exp\left\{-\frac{(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)^{2}Tx^{2}\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})}{3\sigma_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)}\right\}+C\exp\{-c(2+r+2Dr)\}\right)\\
\leq&\exp\left(-C(2+r+2Dr)\right)+C\exp\left(C(2+r+2Dr)-c(2+r+2Dr)\right)\\
\lesssim&C\exp\left(-C(2+r+2Dr)\right).\end{split}

(C.52)

Define

\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}:=2+r+2Dr\text{ and }M_{2}:=\sigma_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/\mu_{\min}^{1/2}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).

(C.53)

Therefore, when
T\gtrsim(4Dr+4)M_{1}^{2}
, we have that with probability at least
1-\\
C\exp\left\{-C(2+r+2Dr)\right\}
,

\xi\lesssim M_{2}\sqrt{(2+r+2Dr)/T}=M_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}}{T}}.

(C.54)

∎

With this condition, we now verify the condition for
\xi
required by Theorem
1
. We can give the following proposition
C.1
.

Proposition C.1
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, if
T\gtrsim(4Dr+4)M_{1}^{2}
as well as

T\gtrsim\phi^{-2}\sigma^{-2}_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})\alpha^{-4}\beta^{2}M_{2}^{2}\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}},

then with probability as least
1-C\exp\left(-CDr\right)
,

\xi\lesssim\alpha^{2}\beta^{-1}\phi\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}).

(C.55)

where
\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}=2+r+2Dr.

Proof of Proposition
C.1
.

By Lemma
C.2
, we know that with high probability,

\xi\lesssim M_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}}{T}}.

(C.56)

Then, when
T\gtrsim\phi^{-2}\sigma^{-2}_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})\alpha^{-4}\beta^{2}M_{2}^{2}\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}
, we have

\xi^{2}\lesssim\frac{M_{2}^{2}\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}}{\phi^{-2}\sigma^{-2}_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})\alpha^{-4}\beta^{2}M_{1}^{2}\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{NLR}}}=\frac{\phi^{2}\sigma^{2}_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})\alpha^{4}}{\beta^{2}}.

(C.57)

∎

C.5
Property of Initialization

Our scaled gradient descent algorithm (Algorithm
1
) requires a high-quality initial estimator
\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)}=(\beta_{\textup{A}}^{(0)},\beta_{\textup{N}}^{(0)},\mathbf{U}^{(0)},\mathbf{V}^{(0)})
that satisfies the conditions specified in Theorem
1
.

We obtain this estimator by solving an optimization problem that directly embeds the known parameter structures
\mathbf{B}_{1}=\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N}
and
\text{rank}(\mathbf{B}_{2})\leq r
. We define our initial estimator
(\beta_{\textup{A}}^{(0)},\beta_{\textup{N}}^{(0)},\mathbf{B}_{2}^{(0)})
as the solution to the following structured optimization problem:

(\beta_{\textup{A}}^{(0)},\beta_{\textup{N}}^{(0)},\mathbf{B}_{2}^{(0)}):=\argmin_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{0},\beta_{1}\in\mathbb{R}\\
\text{rank}(\mathbf{B}_{2})\leq r\end{subarray}}\mathcal{L}_{T}(\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N},\mathbf{B}_{2}),

(C.58)

where
\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{B}_{1},\mathbf{B}_{2})=1/2T\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{B}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
.

Since this problem is non-convex, we compute an effective solution using an Alternating Least Squares (ALS) approach. In practice, a small number of iterations of this procedure is sufficient to generate an estimate
\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)}
that satisfies the required conditions. The procedure alternates between solving two standard least-squares problems:

Solving for
(\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}})
given
\mathbf{B}_{2}
.

For a given
\mathbf{B}_{2}
, the parameters
\boldsymbol{\beta}=(\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}})^{\top}
are found by solving:

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}:=\argmin_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathrm{R}^{2}}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-(\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N})\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(C.59)

This is a standard Ordinary Least Squares (OLS) problem. By vectorizing the model as
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t})=(\mathbf{B}_{2}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{N})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1})+\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{E}_{t})
, using the structural constraint
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1})=\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{struct}}\boldsymbol{\beta}
, where
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{struct}}=[\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{N}),\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{W}_{N})]
, and letting
\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t})
and
\mathbf{Z}_{t}=(\mathbf{B}_{2}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{N})\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{struct}}
, the problem becomes
\min_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{Z}_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}
. The solution is given by:

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}:=\left(\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{Z}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{Z}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}\right).

(C.60)

Solving for
\mathbf{B}_{2}
given
(\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}})
.

For a given
\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}}
, we first reconstruct
\mathbf{B}_{1}=\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N}
. The optimal
\mathbf{B}_{2}
is found by solving a standard Reduced-Rank Regression (RRR) problem
(
Reinsel and Velu, 1998
)
:

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2}:=\argmin_{\text{rank}(\mathbf{B}_{2})\leq r}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{B}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(C.61)

Let
\mathbf{X}_{t}=\mathbf{B}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}
. This problem is equivalent to
\min_{\text{rank}(\mathbf{B}_{2})\leq r}\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{\top}-\mathbf{B}_{2}\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
.
The explicit global solution is given by
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2}=\mathbf{\Gamma}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{\text{OLS}}
.
Here,
\widehat{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{\text{OLS}}=\mathbf{S}_{YX}\mathbf{S}_{XX}^{-1}
is the full-rank OLS estimate, with sample covariance matrices
\mathbf{S}_{YX}=\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{t}
and
\mathbf{S}_{XX}=\sum_{t=2}^{T+1}\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{t}
. The
D\times r
matrix
\mathbf{\Gamma}
contains the
r
leading (normalized) eigenvectors of the
D\times D
matrix
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{S}_{YX}\mathbf{S}_{XX}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{XY}
, where
\mathbf{S}_{XY}=\mathbf{S}_{YX}^{\top}
.
The final solution is thus

\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2}=(\mathbf{\Gamma}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\prime})\mathbf{S}_{YX}\mathbf{S}_{XX}^{-1}.

(C.62)

Final Initial Values

We run a small number of these alternating steps to obtain the final estimates
(\tilde{\beta}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{\beta}_{\textup{N}})
and
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
. We then set
(\beta_{\textup{A}}^{(0)},\beta_{\textup{N}}^{(0)})=(\tilde{\beta}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{\beta}_{\textup{N}})
. Finally, we obtain
\mathbf{U}^{(0)}
and
\mathbf{V}^{(0)}
by computing the rank-
r
SVD of
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}=\mathbf{L}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}
and setting
\mathbf{U}^{(0)}=\mathbf{L}_{r}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{r}^{1/2}
and
\mathbf{V}^{(0)}=\mathbf{R}_{r}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{r}^{1/2}
.

C.6
Auxiliary Lemmas

The next lemma is some conclusions on splitting martices.

Lemma C.3
.

Suppose that there are two
d\times d

\mathrm{rank}
-
r
(meaning that
\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{W}_{1})\leq r
,
\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{W}_{2})\leq r
) matrices
\mathbf{W}_{1}
and
\mathbf{W}_{2}
. Then,
there exists two
d\times d

\mathrm{rank}
-
r
matrices
\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{2}
, such that
\mathbf{W}_{1}+\mathbf{W}_{2}=\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{2}
and
\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\rangle=0
.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

By SVD decomposition we know that there exists
d\times r
orthogonal matrices
\mathbf{U}_{1}
such that
\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{1}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
, and
\mathbf{U}_{2},\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
, such that
\mathbf{W}_{1}=\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}_{2}=\mathbf{U}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top}
. Let

\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{1}=\mathbf{U}_{1}(\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top})
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{2}=(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{U}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top}
, then
\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_{2}
satisfy the conditions.
∎

The following Lemma
C.4
–
C.7
provides useful covering numbers for some sets.

Lemma C.4
.

The
\epsilon
-covering number of the set
\mathcal{R}(d,r):=\{\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\left\|\mathbf{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1,{\mathrm{rank}}(\mathbf{A})\leq r\}
is

|\overline{\mathcal{R}}|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{r+2dr}.

(C.63)

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

we donote
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{L}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}
as the SVD decomposition of the rank-
r
matrix
\mathbf{A}
, where
\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1
and
\mathbf{L},\mathbf{R}
are
d\times r
orthonormal matrixs.
We construct an
\epsilon
-net for
\mathbf{A}
by covering the set of
\mathbf{\Sigma},\mathbf{L}
and
\mathbf{R}
. We take
\overline{\varSigma}
to be an
\epsilon/3
-net for
\mathbf{\Sigma}
with
|\overline{\varSigma}|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{r}.

Next, let
\mathbb{O}^{d\times r}=\{\mathbf{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}:\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{L}=\mathbf{I}_{r}\}
. To cover
\mathbb{O}^{d\times r}
, we consider the
\|\cdot\|_{2,\infty}
norm, defined as

\|\mathbf{X}\|_{2,\infty}=\max_{i}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{2},

(C.64)

where
\mathbf{X}_{i}
is the
i
-th column of
\mathbf{X}
. Let
\mathbb{Q}^{d\times r}=\{\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}:\|\mathbf{X}\|_{2,\infty}\leq 1\}
. It can be easily checked that
\mathbb{O}^{d\times r}\subset\mathbb{Q}^{d\times r}
, and thus an
\epsilon/3
-net
\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{d\times r}
for
\mathbb{O}^{d\times r}
obeying
|\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{d\times r}|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{dr}
.

Denote
\overline{\mathbb{A}}=\{\overline{\mathbf{A}}=\overline{\mathbf{L}}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}:\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\in\overline{\varSigma},~and~\overline{\mathbf{L}},\overline{\mathbf{R}}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{d\times r}\}
and we have

|\overline{\mathbb{A}}|\leq|\overline{\varSigma}|\cdot|\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{d\times r}|\cdot|\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{d\times r}|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{r+2dr}.

(C.65)

It suffices to show that for any
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{R}(d,r)
, there exists a
\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathbb{A}}
such that
\left\|\mathbf{A}-\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\epsilon
.

For any fixed
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{R}(d,r)
, we can get its SVD decomposition
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{L}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}
, then there exist
\overline{\mathbf{A}}=\overline{\mathbf{L}}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}
with
\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\in\overline{\varSigma}
and
\overline{\mathbf{L}},\overline{\mathbf{R}}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{d\times r}
satisfying that
\|{\mathbf{L}-\overline{\mathbf{L}}}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\epsilon/3,\|\mathbf{R}-\overline{\mathbf{R}}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\epsilon/3
and
\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}-\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\epsilon/3
. This gives

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}-\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbf{L}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}-\overline{\mathbf{L}}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(C.66)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\mathbf{L}-\overline{\mathbf{L}})\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\overline{\mathbf{L}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}-\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}})\mathbf{R}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\overline{\mathbf{L}}\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}(\mathbf{R}-\overline{\mathbf{R}})^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{L}-\overline{\mathbf{L}}}\|_{2,\infty}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}-\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\overline{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\|{\mathbf{R}-\overline{\mathbf{R}}}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}=\epsilon.

∎

Lemma C.5
.

Define
\mathcal{R}(N)=\{\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}:\mathbf{R}=\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N},\left\|\mathbf{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1,\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}}\in\mathbb{R}\}
, where
\mathbf{I}_{N}
and
\mathbf{W}_{N}
are defined as before. Let
\overline{\mathcal{R}}(N)
be an
\epsilon
-net of
\mathcal{R}(N)
. Then

|\overline{\mathcal{R}}(N)|\leq(C/\epsilon)^{2}.

(C.67)

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

For any fixed
\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{R}(N)
,
\mathbf{R}=\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N}
, we construct an
\epsilon
-net for
\mathbf{R}
by covering the set of
\beta_{\textup{A}}
and
\beta_{\textup{N}}
. Since
\left\|\mathbf{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1
and
\left\langle\mathbf{I}_{N},\mathbf{W}_{N}\right\rangle=0
, we have

\beta_{\textup{A}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\beta_{\textup{N}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=1.

(C.68)

This is an equation representing an ellipse in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Let
\mathbb{B}=\{\mathbf{b}=(\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}}):\beta_{\textup{A}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\beta_{\textup{N}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=1,\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}}\in\mathbb{R}\}.
We take
\overline{\mathbb{B}}
to be an
\epsilon/c
-net for
\mathbb{B}
, where
c=\text{max}\{\left\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},\left\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\}
. Since an arbitrary ellipsoid can be mapped to the unit ball by a linear transformation,
from Lemma
C.7
, we have
|\overline{\mathbb{B}}|\leq(C/\epsilon)^{2}
, where
C
is a non-zero constant.

Define
\overline{\mathcal{R}}(N)=\{\overline{\mathbf{R}}=\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\overline{\beta}_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N}:(\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}},\overline{\beta}_{\textup{N}})\in\overline{\mathbb{B}}\}
and we have
|\overline{\mathcal{R}}(N)|=|\overline{\mathbb{B}}|\leq(C/\epsilon)^{2}.
It suffices to show that for any
\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{R}(N)
, there exists a
\overline{\mathbf{R}}\in\overline{\mathcal{R}}(N)
such that
\left\|\mathbf{R}-\overline{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\epsilon
.

For any fixed
\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{R}(N)
,
\mathbf{R}=\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N}
, there exist
\overline{\mathbf{R}}=\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\overline{\beta}_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N}
with
(\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}},\overline{\beta}_{\textup{N}})\in\overline{\mathbb{B}}
satisfying
(\beta_{\textup{A}}-\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}})^{2}+(\beta_{\textup{A}}-\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}})^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}/c^{2}.
Then we have

\begin{split}\left\|\mathbf{R}-\overline{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}&=(\beta_{\textup{A}}-\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}})^{2}\left\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+(\beta_{\textup{A}}-\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}})^{2}\left\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq[(\beta_{\textup{A}}-\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}})^{2}+(\beta_{\textup{A}}-\overline{\beta}_{\textup{A}})^{2}]c^{2}\\
&\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{c^{2}}\cdot c^{2}=\epsilon^{2}.\end{split}

(C.69)

∎

Lemma C.6
.

Define the sets of structured matrices without norm constraints:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{1}(N)_{\textup{unc}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}:\mathbf{Q}=\beta_{\textup{A}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N}}\mathbf{W}_{N},~\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}}\in\mathbb{R}\right\},

(C.70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{2}(r,D)_{\textup{unc}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}:\mathbf{Q}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{\top},~\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r}\right\}.

Define the structured Kronecker product space and the set of unit-norm differences:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{\textup{unc}}(r,D;N)&:=\left\{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{1}:\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}(r,D)_{\textup{unc}},\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}(N)_{\textup{unc}}\right\},\\
\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)&:=\left\{\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}:\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{V}_{\textup{unc}}(r,D;N),\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1\right\}.\end{split}

(C.71)

Let
\overline{\mathcal{S}}(r,D;N)
be an
\varepsilon
-covering net of
\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)
with respect to the Frobenius norm, where
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Then

|\overline{\mathcal{S}}(r,D;N)|\leq\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{4Dr+4},

(C.72)

Proof of Lemma
C.6
.

The proof utilizes a volumetric argument based on the intrinsic dimension of the set
\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)
. Although
\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)
resides in the high-dimensional ambient space
\mathbb{R}^{DN\times DN}
, its elements are generated by a low-dimensional parameter space.

We first analyze the degrees of freedom associated with the set
\mathcal{V}(r,D;N)
. Any matrix
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{V}(r,D;N)
is uniquely determined by the parameter tuple
\mathbf{\theta}_{\mathbf{A}}=(\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}},\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V})
, where
\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r}
and
\beta_{\textup{A}},\beta_{\textup{N}}\in\mathbb{R}
. The total number of real-valued parameters required to specify
\mathbf{A}
is
d_{\mathcal{V}}=2Dr+2
. Similarly, any matrix
\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{V}(r,D;N)
is determined by an independent set of parameters with the same degrees of freedom.

Consequently, the unconstrained difference
\mathbf{X}_{\textup{diff}}=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}
lies in the image of a smooth mapping
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{4Dr+4}\to\mathbb{R}^{DN\times DN}
. Observe that
\Phi
is constructed using matrix additions, multiplications, and Kronecker products, which are all polynomial operations with respect to the entries of the parameters. Therefore, the image
\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{Im}(\Phi)
constitutes a semi-algebraic set. According to the Whitney Stratification Theorem in Real Algebraic Geometry
(
Bochnak et al., 2013
)
,
\mathcal{M}
can be decomposed into a finite union of disjoint smooth manifolds, denoted as
\mathcal{M}=\bigcup_{j}\mathcal{M}_{j}
. Since each stratum
\mathcal{M}_{j}
is a subset of the image
\Phi(\mathbb{R}^{4Dr+4})
, its dimension is bounded by the dimension of the domain parameter space. Furthermore, the dimension of a finite union of manifolds is defined as the maximum of the dimensions of its components. Hence, the intrinsic dimension of
\mathcal{M}
is at most:

\dim(\mathcal{M})=\max_{j}\{\dim(\mathcal{M}_{j})\}\leq d_{\textup{total}}=4Dr+4.

(C.73)

The set of interest,
\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)
, is precisely the intersection of this low-dimensional manifold
\mathcal{M}
with the unit Frobenius sphere
\mathbb{S}^{D^{2}N^{2}-1}
:

\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)=\mathcal{M}\cap\left\{\mathbf{X}:\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1\right\}.

(C.74)

A standard result in high-dimensional probability states that, for a set residing on a
k
-dimensional manifold intersected with the unit sphere, the
\varepsilon
-covering number is bounded by
(C/\varepsilon)^{k}
. This result is formalized in the discussion on metric entropy of low-dimensional sets by
Vershynin, 2018
. By substituting the intrinsic dimension
k=4Dr+4
, we obtain the bound

|\overline{\mathcal{S}}(r,N;D)|\leq\left(1+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\right)^{4Dr+4}\leq\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{4Dr+4}.

(C.75)

This completes the proof.
∎

Next lemma is the basic covering result for a unit sphere.

Lemma C.7
.

(
Wainwright, 2019
)

Let
\mathcal{N}
be an
\epsilon
-net of the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{p}
, where
\epsilon\in(0,1]
. Then,

|\mathcal{N}|\leq\left(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\right)^{p}.

(C.76)

The next lemma is a classic result from matrix perturbation theory, also known as the Sin-Theta Theorem, provides a bound on the difference between singular subspaces (and their projectors) based on the matrix estimation error
\|\hat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
and the spectral gap
\delta
.
Crucially, it also provides a bound on the difference between the orthogonal projectors onto these subspaces.

Lemma C.8
.

(
Stewart and Sun, 1990
)

Let
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{A}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
be matrices of rank
r
with singular value decompositions
\mathrm{(SVD)}
:

\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{V}^{\top},\quad\mathbf{A}^{*}=\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{*}(\mathbf{V}^{*})^{\top},

(C.77)

where
\mathbf{U},\mathbf{U}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times r}
and
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{V}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
are column-orthonormal matrices,
\mathbf{\Sigma}=diag(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{r})
and
\mathbf{\Sigma}^{*}=diag(\sigma_{1}^{*},\dots,\sigma_{r}^{*})
contain non-increasing singular values.

Denote the corresponding
r
-dimensional subspaces:

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}&=\textup{spn}(\mathbf{U})\subset\mathbb{R}^{m},\quad\mathcal{U}^{*}=\textup{spn}(\mathbf{U}^{*})\subset\mathbb{R}^{m},\\
\mathcal{V}&=\textup{spn}(\mathbf{V})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad\mathcal{V}^{*}=\textup{spn}(\mathbf{V}^{*})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}.\end{split}

(C.78)

Let
\Theta(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}^{*})
be the matrix of principal angles between
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{U}^{*}
, and define the spectral gap
\delta=\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A}^{*})
. Then the following bounds hold:

\begin{split}\|\sin\Theta(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}^{*})\|_{F}&\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}}{\delta},\\
\|\sin\Theta(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{V}^{*})\|_{F}&\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}}{\delta}.\end{split}

(C.79)

Moreover, the orthogonal projectors
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}^{*}}=\mathbf{U}^{*}(\mathbf{U}^{*})^{\top}
satisfy:

\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}^{*}}\|_{\textup{F}}=\sqrt{2}\|\sin\Theta(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}^{*})\|_{\textup{F}}

(C.80)

Hence, we have

\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}^{*}}\|_{\textup{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\frac{\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}}{\delta}

(C.81)

with identical relations holding for the row space projectors
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{V}}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{V}^{*}}=\mathbf{V}^{*}(\mathbf{V}^{*})^{\top}
.

Appendix D
Consistency of Rank Selection

In this subsection, we give the proof of Theorem
3
.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

We begin by analyzing the theoretical properties of the estimator
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\overline{r})
after applying the optimal scaling,
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{1}(\bar{r})\|_{\text{F}}=\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r})\|_{\text{F}}
.
From Theorem
2
and Lemma
B.2
, with
T\gtrsim M_{1}^{2}D\bar{r}
, we have the estimation error bound for the reduced-rank estimators
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r})
with rank
\bar{r}
:

\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\phi^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\phi^{-1}\alpha^{-1}M_{2}\sqrt{\frac{D\bar{r}}{T}}.

(D.1)

The probability approachs
1
as
T\to\infty
and
D\to\infty
. Since
\text{rank}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})\leq\bar{r}+r
, by the fact that
L_{\infty}
norm is smaller than
L_{2}
norm and Mirsky’s singular value inequality
(
Mirsky, 1960
)
,

\begin{split}\max_{1\leq j\leq\bar{r}+r}|\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))-\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})|^{2}\leq&\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{r}+r}\left(\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))-\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})\right)^{2}\\
\leq&\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{r}+r}\sigma^{2}_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})\\
=&\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
\lesssim&\phi^{-2}\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{D\bar{r}}{T}.\end{split}

(D.2)

Then we know that
\forall j=1,2,...,\bar{r}
,
|\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))-\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})|=O(\phi^{-1}\alpha^{-1}M_{2}\sqrt{D\bar{r}/T})
. Next we will show that as
T,D\to\infty
, the ratio
(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j+1}+s(D,T))/(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}+s(D,T))
achieves its minimum at
j=r
.

For
j>r
,
\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})=0
. Then
\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))=O(\phi^{-1}\alpha^{-1}M_{2}\sqrt{D\bar{r}/T})=o(s(D,T))
. Therefore,

\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))+s(D,T)}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))+s(D,T)}\to 1.

(D.3)

For
j<r
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\to\infty,\\
D\to\infty\end{subarray}}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))+s(D,T)}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))+s(D,T)}

(D.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\to\infty,\\
D\to\infty\end{subarray}}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})+o(s(D,T))+s(D,T)}{\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})+o(s(D,T))+s(D,T)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{D,T\to\infty}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})}{\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})}\leq 1.

For
j=r
,

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))+s(D,T)}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(\bar{r}))+s(D,T)}=

\displaystyle\frac{o(s(D,T))+s(D,T)}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})+o(s(D,T))+s(D,T)}

(D.5)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\frac{s(D,T)}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(\min_{1\leqslant j\leqslant r-1}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})}{\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*})}\right).

Then we know that when
T,D\to\infty
, the ratio will finally achieve its minimum at
j=r
, and the probability of this event converges to 1.
∎

Appendix E
Extension to Lag-
L
Models

This appendix extends the model, estimation methods, and corresponding theory to the general lag-
L
models.

E.1
Algorithm Extension to RRNAR(
L
)

A lag-
L
RRNAR model is

\begin{split}\mathbf{Y}_{t}&=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}\,\mathbf{Y}_{t-\ell}\,\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{\top}+\mathbf{E}_{t}\\
&=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(\beta_{\text{A},\ell}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\text{N},\ell}\mathbf{W}_{N})\,\mathbf{Y}_{t-\ell}\,\mathbf{V}_{\ell}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{\top}+\mathbf{E}_{t},\quad t=1+L,\dots,T+L,\end{split}

(E.1)

where each lag
\ell
is associated with its own node-side coefficients
(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell})
and variable-side reduced-rank factors
(\mathbf{U}_{\ell},\mathbf{V}_{\ell})
.

Consider the observations
\mathbf{Y}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}_{T+L}
generated by the model in (
E.1
), where
T
is the effective sample size for estimation. Assuming rank
\{r_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
is known or pre-specified, our objective is to estimate
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}
, or equivalently the parameters
\mathbf{\Theta}=\{(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell},\mathbf{U}_{\ell},\\
\mathbf{V}_{\ell}),\ell=1,\dots,L\}
. The loss function is

\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta})=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=L+1}^{T+L}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(\beta_{\text{A},\ell}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\text{N},\ell}\mathbf{W}_{N})\mathbf{Y}_{t-\ell}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(E.2)

The ScaledGD algorithm in the Algorithm
E.1
of the main article is proposed for the estimation of RRNAR(
L
) model.

To derive the partial gradients for the lag-
L
model, we first consider its VAR(
L
) representation. Let
\mathbf{y}_{t}=\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t})
,
\mathbf{e}_{t}=\text{vec}(\mathbf{E}_{t})
, and
\mathbf{A}_{\ell}=\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}
. The model in (
E.1
) can be vectorized as

\mathbf{y}_{t}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbf{A}_{\ell}\mathbf{y}_{t-\ell}+\mathbf{e}_{t},\quad t=1+L,\dots,T+L.

(E.3)

We can stack the
T
effective samples into a matrix form. The loss function in (
E.2
) can then be rewritten as a function of the concatenated coefficient matrix
\mathbf{A}
:

\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}):=\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta})=\frac{1}{2T}\left\|\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

(E.4)

where
\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{A}_{L}]
is the
ND\times(LND)
concatenated coefficient matrix. The response matrix
\mathbf{Y}
and the stacked regressor matrix
\mathbf{X}
are defined as:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}

\displaystyle=[\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{T+L}),\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{T+L-1}),\dots,\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{L+1})]\in\mathbb{R}^{ND\times T}

(E.5)

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{T+L-1})&\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{T+L-2})&\cdots&\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{L})\\
\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{T+L-2})&\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{T+L-3})&\cdots&\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{L-1})\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{T})&\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{T-1})&\cdots&\text{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{1})\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(LND)\times T}

The gradient of
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
with respect to the entire concatenated matrix
\mathbf{A}
is

\nabla_{\mathbf{A}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})=-\frac{1}{T}(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X})\mathbf{X}^{\top}.

(E.6)

This total gradient is a block matrix:
\nabla_{\mathbf{A}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}=[\nabla_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{L}},\dots,\nabla_{\mathbf{A}_{L}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}]
. The partial gradient with respect to the
\ell
-th lag block
\mathbf{A}_{\ell}
is

\nabla_{\mathbf{A}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}=-\frac{1}{T}(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X})\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\top},

(E.7)

where
\mathbf{X}_{\ell}
denotes the
\ell
-th block row of
\mathbf{X}
.

For each lag
\ell\in\{1,\dots,L\}
, we utilize the matrix permutation operator
\mathcal{P}
defined in (
A.2
). Let
\mathbf{B}_{\ell}:=\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell})=\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell})\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell})^{\top}
. Following the same derivation as the lag-1 case, the partial gradients for
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}
for each lag
\ell
are:

\begin{split}\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}&=\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla_{\mathbf{A}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}})^{\top}\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell})\right),\\
\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}&=\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla_{\mathbf{A}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}})\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell})\right).\end{split}

(E.8)

Finally, based on the chain rule, the partial gradients of the total loss
\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta})
with respect to the parameters of each lag
\ell
are

\begin{split}\nabla_{\beta_{\text{A},\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta})&=\left\langle\mathbf{I}_{N},\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle,\quad\nabla_{\beta_{\text{N},\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta})=\left\langle\mathbf{W}_{N},\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle,\\
\nabla_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta})&=(\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}})\mathbf{V}_{\ell},\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta})=(\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}^{\top})\mathbf{U}_{\ell}.\end{split}

(E.9)

Algorithm E.1

ScaledGD Algorithm for RRNAR(
L
)

1:

Input:
data
\{\mathbf{Y}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T+L}
,
\mathbf{W}_{N}
,
\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)}
,
\{r_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
,
N,D
, step size
\eta
, and max iteration
I
.

2:

Compute
w=\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}

3:

for

j\leftarrow 0
to
I-1

do

4:

for

\ell\leftarrow 1
to
L

do

5:

\beta_{\text{A},\ell}^{(j+1)}=\beta_{\text{A},\ell}^{(j)}-\eta\cdot\left(\text{tr}(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\cdot N^{-1}\right)\cdot\nabla_{\beta_{\text{A},\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{T}^{(j)}

6:

\beta_{\text{N},\ell}^{(j+1)}=\beta_{\text{N},\ell}^{(j)}-\eta\cdot\left(\text{tr}(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\cdot w^{-1}\right)\cdot\nabla_{\beta_{\text{N},\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{T}^{(j)}

7:

\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}=\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)}-\eta\cdot(\beta_{\text{A},\ell}^{(j)2}N+\beta_{\text{N},\ell}^{(j)2}w)^{-1}\cdot\nabla_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{T}^{(j)}\cdot(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}

8:

\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}=\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j)}-\eta\cdot(\beta_{\text{A},\ell}^{(j)2}N+\beta_{\text{N},\ell}^{(j)2}w)^{-1}\cdot\nabla_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{T}^{(j)}\cdot(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}

9:

end

for

10:

end

for

11:

Return:

\widehat{\mathbf{\Theta}}=\{(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(I)},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{(I)},\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(I)},\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(I)}),\ell=1,\dots,L\}
.

For initialization, we use an effective ALS approach, analogous to the lag-1 case. Specifically, given
\{\mathbf{B}_{\text{net},\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
, we solve all
(\beta_{\text{A},\ell},\beta_{\text{N},\ell})
by ordinary least squares; given
(\beta_{\text{A},\ell},\beta_{\text{N},\ell})
, the problem conveniently decouples into
L
independent reduced-rank regression problems. For each lag
\ell
, we solve for
\mathbf{B}_{\text{var},\ell}
using the standard reduced-rank solution. For rank selection, we can also set a rank upper bound
\bar{r}
for all
r_{\ell}
and then select the ranks by the ridge-type ratio estimator in (
17
). For brevity, the detailed implementations of initialization and rank selection are relegated to Appendix
E.3
.

E.2
Computational Convergence for RRNAR(
L
)

We now generalize the theoretical framework of computational convergence from the lag-1 model to the lag-
L
model. First, we give the equivalence class
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{\Theta})
of all parameter tuples that generate the same model. For the lag-
L
parameter set
\mathbf{\Theta}=\{(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell},\mathbf{U}_{\ell},\mathbf{V}_{\ell})\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{\Theta})=\Big\{\mathbf{\Theta}^{\prime}=\{(\beta^{\prime}_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta^{\prime}_{\textup{N},\ell},\mathbf{U}^{\prime}_{\ell},\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{\ell})\}_{\ell=1}^{L}\ \big|

\displaystyle\beta^{\prime}_{\textup{A},\ell}=(c_{1\ell}c_{2\ell})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\ \beta^{\prime}_{\textup{N},\ell}=(c_{1\ell}c_{2\ell})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{N},\ell},

(E.10)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}^{\prime}_{\ell}=c_{1\ell}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell},\ \mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{\ell}=c_{2\ell}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}^{-\top}

\displaystyle\text{for some}\ c_{1\ell},c_{2\ell}\neq 0,\ \mathbf{Q}_{\ell}\in\mathrm{GL}(r_{\ell}),\ell=1,\dots,L.\Big\}.

The true parameters is defined as
\mathbf{\Theta}^{*}=\{(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{*},\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*},\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*}),\ell=1,\dots,L\}
, where
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}=\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{*}\mathbf{W}_{N}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}=\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*\top}
. For identifiability, we impose a norm-balancing constraint for each lag
\ell=1,\dots,L
,
\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}
, which identifies the pair
(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*})
up to a joint sign switch for each lag. For the given true parameters
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}
, we consider their compact SVDs
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}=\mathbf{L}_{\ell}^{*}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*}{\mathbf{R}_{\ell}^{*}}^{\top}
, and define the factors for each lag as
\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*}:=\mathbf{L}_{\ell}^{*}{\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*}}^{1/2}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*}:=\mathbf{R}_{\ell}^{*}{\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*}}^{1/2}
.

For the metric, we adopt an invariant metric that generalizes the lag-1 distance (
11
) by summing the component-wise distances for each lag
\ell=1,\dots,L
.
Formally, define the squared distance between
\mathbf{\Theta}
and
\mathbf{\Theta}^{*}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}(\mathbf{\Theta},\mathbf{\Theta}^{*})^{2}

(E.11)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mathbf{\Theta}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{\Theta})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\biggl\{\left(\beta_{\text{A},\ell}^{\prime}-\beta_{\text{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\beta_{\text{N},\ell}^{\prime}-\beta_{\text{N},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{\prime}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\prime}-\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}\in\mathrm{GL}(r_{\ell}),\\
c_{1\ell},c_{2\ell}\neq 0,\\
\ell=1,\dots,L\end{subarray}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\biggl\{\left((c_{1\ell}c_{2\ell})^{-1}\beta_{\text{A},\ell}-\beta_{\text{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left((c_{1\ell}c_{2\ell})^{-1}\beta_{\text{N},\ell}-\beta_{\text{N},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|(c_{1\ell}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|(c_{2\ell}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}^{-\top}-\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\biggr\}.

The deviation bound
\xi_{L}
is redefined as the supremum over the entire lag-
L
model space. Specifically, we consider the set of all concatenated matrices that adhere to the model structure and have a unit Frobenius norm. Let
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{L})
be the vector of pre-specified ranks for each lag. We denote this set by
\mathcal{V}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N):=\Big\{\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{A}_{L}]\ \bigg|

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\ell}=(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top})\otimes(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}\mathbf{W}_{N}),

(E.12)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\ell},\mathbf{V}_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r_{\ell}},\ \beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}\in\mathbb{R},\ \forall\ell,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|\mathbf{A}_{\ell}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=1\Big\}.

Using this set, the deviation bound
\xi_{L}
is compactly defined as

\xi_{L}:=\sup_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{V}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\ \mathbf{A}\right\rangle.

(E.13)

With these definitions, we can now state the computational convergence result for the lag-
L
model as follows.

Corollary E.1
.

Suppose that
\mathrm{RSS}
and
\mathrm{RSC}
conditions are satisfied with
\alpha
and
\beta
.
If
\xi_{L}\leq C\min_{\ell}\left\{\phi_{\ell}\sigma_{r_{\ell}}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*})\right\}\alpha^{2}\beta^{-1}
for some universally big constant
C
, the initialization error
\mathrm{dist}(\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)},\mathbf{\Theta}^{*})^{2}
satisfies

\mathrm{dist}(\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)},\mathbf{\Theta}^{*})^{2}\leq C\alpha\beta^{-1}\min_{\ell}\left\{\phi_{\ell}^{2}\sigma_{r_{\ell}}^{2}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*})\right\},

where
C
are sufficiently small constants, and the step size
\eta=\eta_{0}\beta^{-1}
with
\eta_{0}
being a positive constant not greater than
1/64
, then for all
j\geq 1
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathrm{dist}(\mathbf{\Theta}^{(j)},\mathbf{\Theta}^{*})^{2}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1})^{j}\mathrm{dist}(\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)},\mathbf{\Theta}^{*})^{2}+C\eta_{0}\alpha^{-2}\xi_{L}^{2},\\
&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
\lesssim&(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1})^{j}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{(0)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{(0)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\eta_{0}\alpha^{-2}\xi_{L}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.14)

E.2.1
Proof of Corollary
E.1

Step 1
. (Notations and conditions)

We use
\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}
,
\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}
, and
\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}
to represent
\mathrm{dist}(\mathbf{\Theta}^{(j)},\mathbf{\Theta}^{*})^{2}
,
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}
, and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}
defined in main text, respectively. For the
j
-th iterate
\mathbf{\Theta}^{(j)}
, we define the total estimation error as the sum of errors across all
L
lags, minimized over the alignment group:

\begin{split}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}&=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}\in\mathrm{GL}(r_{\ell}),\\
c_{1\ell},c_{2\ell}\neq 0,\\
\ell=1,\dots,L\end{subarray}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\biggl\{\left((c_{1\ell}c_{2\ell})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(j)}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\left((c_{1\ell}c_{2\ell})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{(j)}-\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\|(c_{1\ell}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\|(c_{2\ell}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}^{-\top}-\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\biggr\}.\end{split}

Similar to the lag-1 case, we denote
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}},\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}},\delta_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}},\delta_{\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}}
as the estimation errors for each lag
\ell
under the optimal alignment.

We generalize the RSC, RSS, and RCG conditions (
B.11
) to the lag-
L
model. These conditions now apply to the concatenated
ND\times(LND)
coefficient matrix
\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{A}_{L}]
.

Let
\phi_{\ell}:=\left\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\left\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. We assume that the norm-boundedness condition (
B.14
) holds for each lag
\ell=1,\dots,L
with respect to its own
\phi_{\ell}
, i.e.,:

\begin{gathered}\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq(1+c_{a})\phi_{\ell},\quad\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq(1+c_{a})\phi_{\ell},\\
(1+c_{a})^{-1}\phi_{\ell}^{-1}\leq\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{-1}\leq(1-c_{a})^{-1}\phi_{\ell}^{-1},\\
\text{and }(1+c_{a})^{-1}\phi_{\ell}^{-1}\leq\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{-1}\leq(1-c_{a})^{-1}\phi_{\ell}^{-1},\end{gathered}

(E.15)

where
c_{a}
is constant. We assume that
c_{a}\leq 0.04
, where
0.01
reflects the accuracy of the initial point and can be replaced by any small positive numbers.

Furthermore, we assume an initialization condition analogous to (
B.15
), ensuring the total error
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}
is bounded. Specifically, we require

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}\leq\min\left\{\frac{C_{D}\alpha\min_{\ell}\phi_{\ell}^{4}}{\beta},\frac{C\alpha\min_{\ell}\left\{\phi_{\ell}^{2}\sigma_{r_{\ell}}^{2}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*})\right\}}{\beta}\right\}=\frac{C\alpha\min_{\ell}\left\{\phi_{\ell}^{2}\sigma_{r_{\ell}}^{2}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*})\right\}}{\beta},

(E.16)

for all
j=0,1,2,\dots
. This condition ensures that for all lags
\ell
, the aligned errors are bounded, and there exists a uniform constant
B<1
such that:

\begin{split}&\max\left\{\|(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*-1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}-\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*-1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\}\\
&\leq C\alpha^{1/2}\beta^{-1/2}:=B<1.\quad\forall\ \ell=1,\dots,L.\end{split}

(E.17)

Step 2.
(Upper bound of
\mathrm{dist}_{(j+1)}^{2}-\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}
)

By the optimality of the alignments
c_{1\ell}^{(j+1)}
,
c_{2\ell}^{(j+1)}
, and
\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,(j+1)}
,

\begin{split}&\mathrm{dist}_{(j+1)}^{2}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j+1)}^{2}\\
=&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Big\{\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j+1)}c_{2\ell}^{(j+1)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(j+1)}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&~+\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j+1)}c_{2\ell}^{(j+1)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{(j+1)}-\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&~+\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,(j+1)}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&~+\|(c_{2\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{Q}^{-\top}_{\ell,(j+1)}-\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Big\}\\
\leq&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Big\{\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(j+1)}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&~+\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{(j+1)}-\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&~+\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,(j)}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&~+\|(c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{Q}^{-\top}_{\ell,(j)}-\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Big\}.\end{split}

(E.18)

Step 2.1
(Upper bounds of errors with respect to
\mathbf{U}_{\ell}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\ell}
)

For each lag
\ell
, similar to (
B.21
) in the lag-1 case, we can derive

\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,(j)}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\eta^{2}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},2}-2\eta I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},1},

(E.19)

where

\begin{split}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},2}=&c_{1\ell}^{(j)2}c_{2\ell}^{(j)2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\Big\|\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2c_{1\ell}^{(j)2}c_{2\ell}^{(j)2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\Big\|\text{mat}(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}))\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}))\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+2c_{1\ell}^{(j)2}c_{2\ell}^{(j)2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\\
&~~\Big\|\text{mat}(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}))\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}))\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2},\end{split}

(E.20)

\begin{split}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},1}=&c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\Big\langle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{*1/2},\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}}\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\Big\rangle\\
=&c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\left(G^{(j)}_{2\ell}+G^{(j)}_{1\ell}\right),\end{split}

(E.21)

where

\begin{split}G^{(j)}_{2\ell}=&\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*\top}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}^{\top}\right)\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle,\\
G^{(j)}_{1\ell}=&\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{*}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}-\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*\top}-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}^{\top}\right)\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle.\end{split}

(E.22)

By summing these bounds over all lags
\ell=1,\dots,L
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,(j)}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{*1/2}_{\ell}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\eta^{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},2}^{(j)}-2\eta\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},1}^{(j)}.\end{split}

(E.23)

Step 2.1.1
(Upper bound of
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},2}^{(j)}
)

First, from (
E.20
) we upper bound
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},2}^{(j)}
by

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},2}^{(j)}\\
\leq&2\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\text{mat}(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}))\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}))\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Big\|c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\\
&~~\text{mat}(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}))\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}))\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.24)

For the first term on the RHS of (
E.24
), we donote
C_{\ell}:=c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}
and
\text{mat}_{\ell}:=\text{mat}(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}))\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}))\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}
for convenience. By the definition of Frobenius norm, we have

\begin{split}&2\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\text{mat}(\dots\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*})\dots)\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&2\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|C_{\ell}\cdot\text{mat}_{\ell}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&2\left\|C_{1}\cdot\text{mat}_{1},~C_{2}\cdot\text{mat}_{2},~\ldots,C_{L}\cdot~\text{mat}_{L}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&2\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{W}=[\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{W}_{L}],\\
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=1\end{subarray}}\left\langle\left[C_{1}\cdot\text{mat}_{1},~C_{2}\cdot\text{mat}_{2},~\ldots,C_{L}\cdot~\text{mat}_{L}\right],\mathbf{W}\right\rangle^{2}\\
=&2\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{W}=[\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{W}_{L}],\\
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=1\end{subarray}}\left(\left\langle C_{1}\cdot\text{mat}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{1}\right\rangle+\left\langle C_{2}\cdot\text{mat}_{2},\mathbf{W}_{2}\right\rangle+\ldots+\left\langle C_{L}\cdot\text{mat}_{L},\mathbf{W}_{L}\right\rangle\right)^{2}\\
=&2\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{W}=[\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{W}_{L}],\\
