\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=1\end{subarray}}\Big(\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}),C_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}(\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{1}^{*1/2}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{11}^{(j)}\right\rangle+\ldots\\
&+\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{L}^{*}),C_{L}\mathbf{W}_{L}\mathbf{V}_{L}(\mathbf{V}_{L}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{L})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{L}^{*1/2}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1L}^{(j)}\right\rangle\Big)^{2}.\\
\end{split}

(E.25)

The second equality holds because the total matrix
[C_{1}\cdot\mathrm{mat}_{1},\ldots,C_{L}\cdot\mathrm{mat}_{L}]
is the horizontal concatenation of the disjoint block-wise matrices, and the sum of their squared Frobenius norms is exactly the squared Frobenius norm of the concatenated matrix.
The second-to-last equality uses the fact that, the inner product of concatenated matrices is the sum of the inner products of their respective blocks. Next, by the property in (
E.17
) and Lemma
B.1
, we have
\|\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{op}}\leq(1-B)^{-1}
. Thus, by the inequality
\|\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\text{op}}\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\text{F}}
, we further upper bound (
E.25
) as

\begin{split}&2\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\text{mat}(\dots\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*})\dots)\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2(1-B)^{-2}\cdot\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{W}=[\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{W}_{L}],\\
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=1\end{subarray}}\Big(\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}),\mathbf{W}_{1}\otimes C_{1}\mathbf{B}_{11}^{(j)}\right\rangle+\ldots\\
&\qquad+\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{L}^{*}),\mathbf{W}_{L}\otimes C_{L}\mathbf{B}_{1L}^{(j)}\right\rangle\Big)^{2}\\
=&2(1-B)^{-2}\cdot\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{W}=[\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{W}_{L}],\\
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=1\end{subarray}}\Big(\left\langle\left[\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}),\ldots,\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{L}^{*})\right],\right.\\
&\qquad\left.\left[\mathbf{W}_{1}\otimes C_{1}\mathbf{B}_{11}^{(j)},\ldots,\mathbf{W}_{L}\otimes C_{L}\mathbf{B}_{1L}^{(j)}\right]\right\rangle\Big)^{2}\\
\leq&2(1-B)^{-2}\cdot\left\|\left[\mathbf{W}_{1}\otimes C_{1}\mathbf{B}_{11}^{(j)},\ldots,\mathbf{W}_{L}\otimes C_{L}\mathbf{B}_{1L}^{(j)}\right]\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\xi_{L}^{2}\\
\leq&2(1-B)^{-2}\cdot\max_{\ell}\|C_{\ell}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\xi_{L}^{2}\\
=&2(1-B)^{-2}\max_{\ell}\{\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\}\cdot\xi_{L}^{2},\end{split}

(E.26)

For the second term on the RHS of (
E.24
), which involves the gradient difference, we first bound the inner matrix F-norm for each
\ell
. Let
\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell}:=\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*})
. We have

\begin{split}&\left\|\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell})\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)})\right)\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&\left\|\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell})\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)})\right)\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{V}_{\ell}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\\
\leq&\left\|\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot(1-B)^{-2},\end{split}

(E.27)

where the second inequality uses the property
\|\text{mat}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{M})\text{vec}(\mathbf{N}))\|_{\text{F}}\leq\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\text{F}}\|\mathbf{N}\|_{\text{F}}
and the bound
B<1
from (
E.17
). Now, we substitute this back into the second term of (
E.24
) and sum over
\ell
:

\begin{split}&2\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\text{mat}(\dots\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell}\dots)\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2(1-B)^{-2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left(\left\|\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\\
=&2(1-B)^{-2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&2(1-B)^{-2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\end{split}

(E.28)

This is a weighted sum of the squared Frobenius norms of the gradient difference blocks. To relate this to the global gradient difference
\nabla_{\text{diff}}:=\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})
, we bound the weights by their maximum value:

\begin{split}&2\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\text{mat}(\dots\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell}\dots)\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq 2(1-B)^{-2}\cdot\max_{\ell=1,\dots,L}\left\{\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right\}\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|\nabla_{\text{diff},\ell}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\\
&=2(1-B)^{-2}\cdot\max_{\ell=1,\dots,L}\left\{\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right\}\cdot\left\|\nabla_{\text{diff}}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.29)

The final equality holds by the definition of the Frobenius norm. Since the total gradient difference matrix
\nabla_{\text{diff}}=[\nabla_{\text{diff},1},\dots,\nabla_{\text{diff},L}]
is the horizontal concatenation of the disjoint block-wise gradient difference matrices.

Combining (
E.26
) and (
E.29
), we obtain the final upper bound for
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},2}^{(j)}
,

\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},2}^{(j)}\leq 2(1-B)^{-2}\max_{\ell}\{\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\}\cdot(\xi_{L}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\text{diff}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}):=R_{\mathbf{U},2}^{(j)}.

(E.30)

Step 2.1.2
(Lower bound of
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},1}^{(j)}
)

We now lower bound the sum
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},1}^{(j)}
. From (
E.21
) we have

\begin{split}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},1}^{(j)}&=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\left(G^{(j)}_{2\ell}+G^{(j)}_{1\ell}\right)\\
&\geq\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)G^{(j)}_{2\ell}-\left|\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)G^{(j)}_{1\ell}\right|,\end{split}

(E.31)

where
G^{(j)}_{1\ell}
and
G^{(j)}_{2\ell}
are defined in (
E.22
).
Denote
C_{\ell}:=c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
. The term
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}C_{\ell}G^{(j)}_{2\ell}
keeps fixed. We now derive a uniform upper bound for the term,
|\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}C_{\ell}G^{(j)}_{1\ell}|
.

Following the same steps as in the lag-1 proof ((
B.27
) and (
B.28
) i.e., splitting
\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})
in
|G^{(j)}_{1}|
via triangle inequality and bounding the
\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})
component with
\xi
and the
\nabla_{\text{diff}}
component with Cauchy–Schwarz), while employing the same concatenation matrix technique from
Step 2.1.1
, we can obtain

\begin{split}&|\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}C_{\ell}G^{(j)}_{1\ell}|\\
=&\left|\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}C_{\ell}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{*}(\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\ell})^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top}-\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*\top}-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}^{\top}\right)\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\right|\\
\leq&\left(\xi_{L}+\|\nabla_{\text{diff}}\|_{\text{F}}\right)\cdot\left[\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2}\right)^{2}\|C_{\ell}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
=&\left(\xi_{L}+\|\nabla_{\text{diff}}\|_{\text{F}}\right)\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2}\right)\cdot\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(E.32)

Hence, from (
E.22
) and (
E.32
), we obtain the following lower bound for
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},1}
,

\begin{split}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell},1}^{(j)}\geq&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Bigg[\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\\
&~~\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*\top}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}^{\top}\right)\otimes(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\Bigg]\\
&-\left(\xi_{L}+\|\nabla_{\text{diff}}\|_{\text{F}}\right)\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2}\right)\\
&~\cdot\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
:=&R_{\mathbf{U},1}^{(j)}.\end{split}

(E.33)

Combining the results from
Step 2.1.1
and
Step 2.1.2
, we have the following upper bound for the summation over all lags
\ell
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,(j)}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,(j)}-\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq\eta^{2}R_{\mathbf{U},1}^{(j)}-2\eta R_{\mathbf{U},2}^{(j)}.\end{split}

(E.34)

where
R_{\mathbf{U},1}
and
R_{\mathbf{U},2}
are defined in (
E.33
) and (
E.30
), respectively.

Similarly, for
\mathbf{V}_{\ell}
, we can also define the quantities and show that

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j+1)}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,(j)}^{-\top}-\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{2\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(j)}\mathbf{Q}^{-\top}_{\ell,(j)}-\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq\eta^{2}R_{\mathbf{V},1}^{(j)}-2\eta R_{\mathbf{V},2}^{(j)}.\end{split}

(E.35)

Step 2.2
(Upper bounds of errors with respect to
\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}
and
\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}
)

For each lag
\ell
, similar to (
B.33
) in the lag-1 case, we can derive

\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(j+1)}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\mathbf{\delta}^{2}_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\eta^{2}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},2}^{(j)}-2\eta I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},1}^{(j)}.

(E.36)

where

\begin{split}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},2}^{(j)}&=(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-2}\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\left\langle\mathbf{I}_{N},\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}))^{\top}\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)})\right)\right\rangle^{2}\\
&=(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-2}\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\left\langle\mathbf{I}_{N},\text{diag}\left\{\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}))^{\top}\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)})\right)\right\}\right\rangle^{2}\\
&\leq(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\left\|\text{diag}\left\{\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}))^{\top}\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)})\right)\right\}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\end{split}

(E.37)

I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},1}^{(j)}=\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\mathbf{\delta}_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}}\cdot\Big\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{N}\Big\rangle

(E.38)

By summing these bounds over all lags
\ell=1,\ldots,L
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(j+1)}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbf{\delta}^{2}_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\eta^{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},2}^{(j)}-2\eta\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},1}^{(j)}.\end{split}

(E.39)

Step 2.2.1
(Upper bound of
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},2}^{(j)}
)

First, from (
E.37
), we upper bound
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},2}^{(j)}
by

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},2}^{(j)}\\
\leq&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\left\|\text{diag}\left\{\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)}))^{\top}\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)})\right)\right\}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\left\|\text{diag}\left\{\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}))^{\top}\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)})\right)\right\}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-4}\cdot\left\|\text{diag}\left\{\text{mat}\left(\mathcal{P}(\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}))^{\top}\text{vec}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)})\right)\right\}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.40)

By employing the same concatenation matrix technique from
Step 2.1.1
, we can derive

\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},2}^{(j)}\leq 2\max_{\ell}\{\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\}\cdot(\xi^{2}+\|\nabla_{\text{diff}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}):=R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},2}^{(j)}.

(E.41)

Step 2.2.2
(Lower bound of
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},1}^{(j)}
)

From (
E.38
), we can derive the following lower bound for
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},1}^{(j)}
,

\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}I_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},1}^{(j)}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\mathbf{\delta}_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}}\cdot\Big\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{N}\Big\rangle:=R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}.

(E.42)

Combining the results from
Step 2.2.1
and
Step 2.2.2
, we have the following upper bound for the summation over all lags
\ell
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(j+1)}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(j)}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq\eta^{2}R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}-2\eta R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},2}^{(j)}.\end{split}

(E.43)

where
R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}
and
R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},2}^{(j)}
are defined in (
E.42
) and (
E.41
), respectively.

Similarly, we can also derive the following upper bound for the errors with respect to
\beta_{\textup{N}}
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{(j+1)}-\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{(j)}-\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{*}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq\eta^{2}R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},1}^{(j)}-2\eta R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},2}^{(j)}.\end{split}

(E.44)

Combining the inequalities in (
E.34
), (
E.35
), (
E.43
), and (
E.44
), according to (
E.18
), we
obtain the following upper bound of
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}
:

\begin{split}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}\leq&\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}+\eta^{2}(R_{\mathbf{U},2}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},2}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},2}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},2}^{(j)})\\
&-2\eta(R_{\mathbf{U},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},1}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},1}^{(j)}).\end{split}

(E.45)

Step 3.
(Combined upper bound of
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}
)

In this step, we derive lower bounds of the sum of
R^{(j)}_{\cdot,1}
, and upper bounds of the sum of
R_{\cdot,2}^{(j)}
. Finally we obtain an upper bound as in (
B.55
). In
Step 3.1
, we find the lower bound of
R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{U},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},1}^{(j)}
. In
Step 3.2
, we find the upper bound of
R^{(j)}_{\beta_{\textup{A}},2}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},2}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{U},2}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},2}^{(j)}
.

Step 3.1
(Lower bound of
R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{U},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},1}^{(j)}
)

According to (
E.42
), (
E.33
), and its analogue for
R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},1}^{(j)}
and
R_{\mathbf{V},1}^{(j)}
, we have

\begin{split}&R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{U},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},1}^{(j)}\\
=&\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{-2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\Big\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes(\mathbf{\delta}_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\mathbf{\delta}_{\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}}\mathbf{W}_{N})\Big\rangle\\
&+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Bigg[\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\\
&~~~\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*\top}+\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}+\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}^{\top}\right)\otimes(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\Bigg]\\
&-\left(\xi_{L}+\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\right)\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2}\right)\cdot\\
&~~~\Biggl[\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\qquad+\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Biggr]\\
&:=P_{1}^{(j)}-P_{2}^{(j)}.\end{split}

(E.46)

First, we focus on the term
P_{1}^{(j)}
. By the condition (
E.15
), we have

\begin{split}P_{1}^{(j)}\geq&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Big[\Big\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes(\mathbf{\delta}_{\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\mathbf{\delta}_{\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}}\mathbf{W}_{N})\Big\rangle\\
&+\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{*\top}+\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{*}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}+\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}^{\top}\right)\otimes(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\Big]\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\right)\right\rangle\\
&+\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\right)\otimes(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\Big]\end{split}

(E.47)

We denote
\mathbf{H}_{1\ell}^{(j)}
as the first order perturbation of
\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}
from the true value:

\mathbf{H}_{1\ell}^{(j)}:=\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\right)\otimes\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right)+\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\right)\otimes\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\right).

(E.48)

By Lemma
B.2
,
\mathbf{H}_{1\ell}^{(j)}
can be expressed as

\mathbf{H}_{1\ell}^{(j)}=\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}+\mathbf{H}_{\ell}^{(j)},

(E.49)

where
\mathbf{H}_{\ell}^{(j)}
represents the higher-order perturbation terms in
\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}
, satisfying
\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}/{2}\cdot(2+B)\phi_{\ell}^{-2}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}
, and
B
is error bound defined in (
E.17
).

Hence, we have

\begin{split}P_{1}^{(j)}=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)}),\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}+\mathbf{H}_{\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Big[\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}),\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\right\rangle\\
&+\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}),\mathbf{H}_{\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}),\mathbf{H}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\Big]\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\mathbf{A}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\right\rangle\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}),\mathbf{H}_{\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}),\mathbf{H}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\\
=:&P_{11}^{(j)}+P_{12}^{(j)}+P_{13}^{(j)},\end{split}

(E.50)

where the third equality follows from the fact that, the inner product of concatenated matrices is the sum of the inner products of their respective block.
For
P_{12}^{(j)}
, by applying the same processing method as lag 1 in (
B.47
), we can obtain

\begin{split}P_{12}^{(j)}\geq&-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\frac{1}{4\beta}\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(2+B)^{2}\alpha C_{D}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}\right)\\
=&-\frac{1}{8\beta}\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}(2+B)^{2}\alpha C_{D}\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2},\end{split}

(E.51)

where the equality from the definition of
\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}=\sum_{\ell}^{L}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}
in (
E.2.1
). For
P_{13}^{(j)}
, by Lemma
B.2
, condition (
E.15
), the definition of
\xi
in (
E.13
), and the fact that, the inner product of concatenated matrices is the sum of the inner products of their respective block, we have

\begin{split}|P_{13}^{(j)}|=&\left|\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}),\mathbf{H}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\rangle\right|\\
\leq&\left|\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}),\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\right)\otimes\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right)\right\rangle\right|\\
&+\left|\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}_{\ell}^{*}),\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\right)\otimes\left((c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\right)\right\rangle\right|\\
\leq&\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\cdot\xi_{L}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\left\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}-\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\cdot\xi_{L}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\frac{1}{2}(2+B)^{2}\phi_{\ell}^{-2}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}\cdot 2\phi_{\ell}^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\phi_{\ell}^{-2}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}\cdot 2\phi_{\ell}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right]\cdot\xi_{L}\\
=&\frac{1}{2}(2+B+\sqrt{2})\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}\xi_{L}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4}(2+B+\sqrt{2})\left(a_{1}\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}+\frac{1}{a_{1}}\xi_{L}^{2}\right),\end{split}

(E.52)

where the second-to-last line use the relation that,
\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}=\sum_{\ell}^{L}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}
in (
E.2.1
), and the last inequality comes from the fact that
x^{2}+y^{2}\geq 2xy
, and
a_{1}
can be any non-zero constant.
Combining (
E.50
), (
E.51
), and (
E.52
), together with the RCG condition in (
B.11
) and Lemma
B.4
, we have the following lower bound for
P_{1}
:

\begin{split}P_{1}^{(j)}\geq&\frac{\alpha}{4}\left\|\mathbf{A}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\beta}\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&-\frac{1}{8\beta}\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}(2+B)^{2}\alpha C_{D}\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}\\
&-\frac{1}{4}(2+B+\sqrt{2})\left(a_{1}\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}+\frac{1}{a_{1}}\xi_{L}^{2}\right)\\
=&\left(\frac{\alpha}{4(\sqrt{2}+1)^{2}}-\frac{1}{4}(2+B)^{2}\alpha C_{D}-\frac{1}{4}(2+B+\sqrt{2})a_{1}\right)\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}\\
&+\frac{1}{8\beta}\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{4a_{1}}(2+B+\sqrt{2})\right)\xi_{L}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.53)

Next, we focus on the term
P_{2}^{(j)}
. By applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and condition (
E.15
), we have

\begin{split}P_{2}^{(j)}=&\left(\xi_{L}+\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\right)\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2}\right)\cdot\\
&~~~\Biggl[\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\qquad+\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Biggr]\\
\leq&\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2}\right)\Bigg[\frac{1}{a_{2}}\left(\xi_{L}+\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\right)^{2}\\
&+2a_{2}\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{U}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|(c_{1\ell}^{(j)}c_{2\ell}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{-2}\|\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}\cdot\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbf{V}_{\ell}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{*1/2}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Bigg)\Bigg]\\
\leq&\frac{2}{a_{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2}\right)\left(\xi_{L}^{2}+\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)+4a_{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2}\right)\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2},\end{split}

(E.54)

where
a_{2}
can be any positive constant.

Combining (
E.46
), (
E.53
), and (
E.54
), we have the following lower bound for
R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{U},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},1}^{(j)}
:

\begin{split}&R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{U},1}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},1}^{(j)}\\
\geq&\left(\frac{\alpha}{4(\sqrt{2}+1)^{2}}-\frac{1}{4}(2+B)^{2}\alpha C_{D}-\frac{1}{4}(2+B+\sqrt{2})a_{1}-4a_{2}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})\right)\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}\\
&+\left(\frac{1}{8\beta}-\frac{2}{a_{2}}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})\right)\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&-\left(\frac{1}{4a_{1}}(2+B+\sqrt{2})+\frac{2}{a_{2}}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})\right)\xi_{L}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.55)

Step 3.2
(Upper bound of
R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},2}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},2}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{U},2}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},2}^{(j)}
)

Following (
E.41
), (
E.30
), and its analogue for
R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},2}^{(j)}
and
R_{\mathbf{V},2}^{(j)}
, together with condition (
E.15
), we have

\begin{split}&R_{\beta_{\textup{A}},2}^{(j)}+R_{\beta_{\textup{N}},2}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{U},2}^{(j)}+R_{\mathbf{V},2}^{(j)}\\
=&4\max_{\ell}\left\{\left\|\mathbf{B}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\left\|(c_{1}^{(j)}c_{2}^{(j)})\mathbf{B}_{2}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{-2}\right\}\cdot\left(\xi_{L}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\\
&+4(1-B)^{-2}\max_{\ell}\left\{\left\|\mathbf{B}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\left\|(c_{1}^{(j)}c_{2}^{(j)})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{-2}\right\}\cdot\left(\xi_{L}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\\
\leq&\left(8+8(1-B)^{-2}\right)\left(\xi_{L}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).\end{split}

(E.56)

So far, we have derived the bounds of all parts of (
E.45
), namely the lower bounds in (
E.55
), and upper bounds in (
E.56
). Combining them, we have

\begin{split}&\mathrm{dist}_{(j+1)}^{2}\\
\leq&\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}+\eta^{2}\left(8+8(1-B)^{-2}\right)\left(\xi^{2}+\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\\
&-2\eta\Bigg\{\Bigg[\frac{\alpha}{4(\sqrt{2}+1)^{2}}-4a_{2}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})-\frac{1}{4}(2+B)^{2}\alpha C_{D}-\frac{a_{1}}{4}\left(2+B+\sqrt{2}\right)\Bigg]\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}\\
&+\bigg[\frac{1}{8\beta}-\frac{2}{a_{2}}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})\bigg]\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
&-\bigg[\frac{2}{a_{2}}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})+\frac{1}{4a_{1}}\left(2+B+\sqrt{2}\right)\bigg]\xi_{L}^{2}\Bigg\}\\
=&\left\{1-2\eta\Bigg[\frac{\alpha}{4(\sqrt{2}+1)^{2}}-4a_{2}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})-\frac{1}{4}(2+B)^{2}\alpha C_{D}-\frac{a_{1}}{4}\left(2+B+\sqrt{2}\right)\Bigg]\right\}\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}\\
&+\eta\bigg[8\eta+\frac{8\eta}{(1-B)^{2}}+\frac{4}{a_{2}}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})-\frac{1}{4\beta}\bigg]\left\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\\
&+\eta\bigg[8\eta+\frac{8\eta}{(1-B)^{2}}+\frac{4}{a_{2}}(\frac{\sqrt{2}B}{1-B}+\frac{B}{2})+\frac{1}{2a_{1}}\left(2+B+\sqrt{2}\right)\bigg]\xi_{L}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.57)

Step 4.
(Recursive relationship between
\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}
and
\mathrm{dist}_{(j+1)}^{2}
)

We start from the recursive inequality (
E.57
). The subsequent derivation of the final recursive relationship is perfectly analogous to the lag-1 analysis (
Step 5
in the Section
B.1
).

We make the same assignments for
\eta
and
a_{1}
as in the lag-1 case:

\eta=\frac{\eta_{0}}{\beta}\quad\text{and}\quad a_{1}=\alpha C_{D}.

(E.58)

For
a_{2}
, we set:

a_{2}=\alpha^{1/2}\beta^{1/2}.

(E.59)

Note that this assignment for
a_{2}
differs from the lag-1 case, which included a factor of
\phi
. This simpler assignment is justified because the
\phi^{-1}
term, present in the
\mathrm{dist}_{(j)}^{2}
coefficient of the lag-1 inequality, has been absorbed in our lag-
L
derivation, resulting in the ”cleaner” coefficient seen in (
E.57
).

With these assignments, the coefficients of
\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
,
\xi_{L}^{2}
, and
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}
are bounded in exactly the same manner as in the lag-1 proof. This leads to the identical recursive relationship:

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}\leq\left(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\right)\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}+C\eta_{0}\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\xi_{L}^{2},

(E.60)

where
C
is a generic positive constant. This yields the geometric convergence

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1})^{j}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}+C\eta_{0}\alpha^{-2}\xi_{L}^{2}.

(E.61)

Step 5.
(Verification of the conditions)

The verification of conditions (
E.15
) and (
E.16
) follows a recursive argument that is perfectly analogous to the lag-1 proof (
Step 6
in Section
B.1
).

The logic proceeds identically:

1.

For
j=0
, the initialization condition (
E.16
) is assumed to hold. Because
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}
bounds the total error, it a fortiori bounds the error of each individual lag
\ell
.

2.

This allows the verification of the norm-boundedness condition (
E.15
) for
j=0
for all
\ell=1,\dots,L
with respect to its own
\phi_{\ell}
, following the same algebraic steps as in the lag-1 case.

3.

Assuming (
E.15
) and (
E.16
) hold for iterate
j
, the final recursive relationship (
E.60
) and condition
\xi_{L}^{2}\leq C\min_{\ell}\left\{\phi_{\ell}^{2}\sigma_{r_{\ell}}^{2}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*})\right\}\alpha^{4}\beta^{-2}
ensures that
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}
also satisfies the bound in (
E.16
).

4.

This in turn allows the verification of (
E.15
) for the
(j+1)
-th iterate.

The induction is thus finished, and the proof is complete.

E.3
Theoretical Guarantees for RRNAR(
L
)

This section extends the entire statistical guarantee framework developed for the lag-1 model (Sections
C.1
to
C.5
) to the general RRNAR(
L
) model. The following theorem is the lag-
L
counterpart to Theorem
2
.

Theorem E.1

(Statistical Rate for RRNAR(
L
))
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, if the sample size
T\gtrsim M_{1}^{2}Dr_{\textup{total}}
,
\textup{dist}(\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)},\mathbf{\Theta}^{*})\leq C\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}\beta_{\textup{RSS}}^{-1}\min_{\ell}\{\phi_{\ell}^{2}\sigma_{r_{\ell}}^{2}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*})\}
, where
\phi_{\ell}=\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}=\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}
, then with probability at least
1-C\exp(-CDr_{\textup{total}})
, the ScaledGD output in Algorithm
E.1
after sufficiently many iterations
J
satisfies

\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{(J)}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{(J)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{Dr_{\textup{total}}}{T},

(E.62)

where
r_{\textup{total}}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}r_{\ell}
.

Theorem
E.1
establishes the convergence rate for the lag-
L
model. The convergence rate is governed by the total effective dimension
Dr_{\text{total}}
relative to the sample size
T
. Crucially, similar to the lag-1 case, this rate remains independent of the number of nodes
N
. This confirms that the proposed RRNAR(
L
) model successfully averts the curse of dimensionality even when incorporating multiple time lags, maintaining scalability for high-dimensional network time series.

To derive component-wise guarantees from the overall error bound in Theorem
E.1
, we must address the identifiability issues analogous to those in the lag-1 setting. For each lag
\ell\in\{1,\dots,L\}
, the parameters suffer from scale ambiguity between
\mathbf{B}_{\text{var},\ell}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\text{net},\ell}
. We resolve this by enforcing the lag-specific constraints
\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\text{var},\ell}\|_{\textup{F}}=\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\text{net},\ell}\|_{\textup{F}}
and letting
\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A},\ell}>0
. Furthermore, to handle factorization ambiguity in
\mathbf{B}_{\text{var},\ell}
, we evaluate the estimation accuracy of the singular subspaces via orthogonal projectors onto the column spaces of
\mathbf{U}_{\ell}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\ell}
. The following corollary provides bounds for these identifiable quantities for each lag.

Corollary E.2

(Component-wise Rates for RRNAR(
L
))
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
E.1
, let
\{(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A},\ell},\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{N},\ell}),\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\textup{var},\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
be the final estimators obtained after
J\geq J_{0}
iterations, where
J_{0}
is a sufficiently large constant. Then, with probability at least
1-C\exp(-CDr_{\textup{total}})
, the estimator returned by ScaledGD for each lag
\ell\in\{1,\dots,L\}
is bounded by:

\displaystyle(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A},\ell}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\phi_{\ell}^{-2}\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{Dr_{\textup{total}}}{NT},

(E.63)

\displaystyle(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{N},\ell}-\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\phi_{\ell}^{-2}\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{Dr_{\textup{total}}}{\left\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\right\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}T},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\ell}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}^{*}_{\ell}}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\phi_{\ell}^{-2}\sigma_{r_{\ell}}^{-2}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*})\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{Dr_{\textup{total}}}{T},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\ell}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{V}^{*}_{\ell}}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\phi_{\ell}^{-2}\sigma_{r_{\ell}}^{-2}(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}^{*})\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{Dr_{\textup{total}}}{T}.

Corollary
E.2
demonstrates that the “blessing of dimensionality” observed in the lag-1 model extends to the multi-lag scenario. For every lag
\ell
, the estimation errors for the scalar coefficients
\widehat{\beta}_{\text{A},\ell}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{\text{N},\ell}
scale inversely with the network size
N
(assuming
\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{F}^{2}
grows with
N
), indicating improved statistical accuracy for larger networks. Meanwhile, the estimation of the singular subspaces for each lag is robust to the network size, depending primarily on the effective dimension
Dr_{\text{total}}
and sample size
T
.

We follow an analogous logical structure, demonstrating that all core results hold for the lag-
L
case through a direct and analogous argument. Section
E.3.1
provides the proofs for the main statistical error rates (Theorem
E.1
and Corollary
E.2
). The subsequent subsections establish the key components required for this proof: the RSC and RSS conditions (Section
E.3.2
), the high-probability bound on the statistical deviation
\xi_{L}
(Section
E.3.3
), and the property of the initialization (Section
E.3.4
).

The fundamental proof structure, which relies on concentration inequalities over the parameter manifold, remains the same. As shown in the following subsections, the key modification is replacing the lag-1 model complexity (
\mathrm{df_{NLR}}
in Lemma
C.2
) with the total degrees of freedom of the lag-
L
model
\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}
, which is the sum of the complexities of each individual lag. This confirms that the statistical properties scale gracefully with the number of lags,
L
.

E.3.1
Proofs of Theorem
E.1
and Corollary
E.2

This section provides the proofs for Theorem
E.1
and Corollary
E.2
, which establish the statistical error rates for the RRNAR(
L
) model. And the framework and logic of the proofs are entirely analogous to the case of lag1.

Proof of Theorem
E.1
.

The proof is perfectly analogous to the proof of Theorem
2
(the lag-1 case) in Section
C.1
. The logic integrates the computational and statistical results for the lag-
L
model.

First, we invoke the high-probability guarantees for the lag-
L
model. Lemma
E.1
and Lemma
E.2
establish the validity of the RSC and RSS conditions and the statistical deviation bound
\xi_{L}\lesssim M_{2}\sqrt{\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}/T}
. We also use the initialization procedure from Section
E.3.4
, which, by an analysis analogous to the lag-1 case, yields an initial
\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)}
satisfying the
\mathcal{O}(1)
conditions required by Corollary
E.1
.

We then apply the computational convergence result from Corollary
E.1
:

\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1})^{j}\mathrm{dist}_{(0)}^{2}+C\eta_{0}\alpha^{-2}\xi_{L}^{2}.

(E.64)

For a sufficiently large number of constant iterations
J\geq J_{0}
, the first term (computational error) decays geometrically. The total error is therefore dominated by the statistical error term,
\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}\lesssim C\eta_{0}\alpha^{-2}\xi_{L}^{2}
. Substituting the bound for
\xi_{L}^{2}
from Lemma
E.2
gives the total distance bound:

\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}\lesssim\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}}{T}.

(E.65)

Finally, by the equivalence established in the generalized Lemma
B.4
, this squared distance
\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}
is equivalent to the squared Frobenius norm of the total error,
\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}||\dots||_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Corollary
E.2
.

The proof is analogous to that of Corollary
1
in Section
C.2
.

We start from the final statistical bound derived in Theorem
E.1
, which states that the total estimation error is bounded:

\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}\lesssim\alpha^{-2}M_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}}{T}.

(E.66)

The total lag-
L
distance is defined as the sum of non-negative individual lag distances,
\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}
in (
E.2.1
). This implies that the error for any individual lag
\ell
must also be bounded by the total error:
\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}\leq\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}
.

For the bounds on
\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}
and
\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}
, we use the definition of
\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}
, which gives
(\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A},\ell}-\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{*})^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}\leq\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}
. Substituting
\|\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=N
,
\|\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}||_{\mathrm{F}}=\phi_{\ell}
, and the final bound for
\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}
yields the result for
\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A},\ell}
.The bound for
\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{N},\ell}
follows identically.

For the bounds on the projectors, we first use the generalized version of Lemma
B.2
to bound the individual matrix error:
\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2\ell}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}||_{F}^{2}\leq C\phi_{\ell}^{-2}\mathrm{dist}_{\ell,(j)}^{2}
. We then apply Lemma
C.8
to relate this to the projector error:

\|\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\ell}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}^{*}_{\ell}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2\sigma_{r_{\ell}}^{-2}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*})\cdot||\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2\ell}-\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}^{*}||_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(E.67)

Combining these inequalities and substituting the final bound for
\mathrm{dist}_{(J)}^{2}
yields the desired rates for the projectors.
∎

E.3.2
Verification of RSC and RSS conditions for RRNAR(
L
)

We now generalize the RSC and RSS verification from the lag-1 model (Section
C.3
) to the lag-
L
case.
Recall the vectorized lag-
L
model defined in (
E.4
):

\mathbf{y}_{t}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbf{A}_{\ell}\mathbf{y}_{t-\ell}+\mathbf{e}_{t},\quad t=L+1,\dots,T+L,

(E.68)

where
\mathbf{A}_{\ell}=\mathbf{B}_{\textup{var},\ell}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\textup{net},\ell}
. We define the
ND\times(LND)
concatenated coefficient matrix
\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{A}_{L}]
and the
LND\times 1
stacked regressor vector
\mathbf{x}_{t-1}=[\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\dots,\mathbf{y}_{t-L}^{\top}]^{\top}
. The model can then be compactly written as

\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{t-1}+\mathbf{e}_{t},\quad t=L+1,\dots,T+L.

(E.69)

The least squares loss function for the
T
effective samples is

\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=L+1}^{T+L}\left\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{t-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(E.70)

It is easy to check that for any
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{A}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{ND\times LND}
adhering to the model structure,

\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})-\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})-\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\rangle=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=L}^{T+L-1}\left\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*})\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(E.71)

This quadratic form is structurally identical to the lag-1 case (
C.23
), where the concatenated matrix
\mathbf{A}
replaces the single
\mathbf{A}
, and the stacked regressor
\mathbf{x}_{t}
replaces
\mathbf{y}_{t}
. We first define the structured Kronecker product space without the unit-norm constraint for the lag-
L
model:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{L}_{\text{unc}}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N):=\Big\{\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{A}_{L}]\ \bigg|

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\ell}=(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top})\otimes(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}\mathbf{W}_{N}),

(E.72)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\ell},\mathbf{V}_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r_{\ell}},\ \beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}\in\mathbb{R},\ \forall\ell\Big\}.

We can therefore establish the RSC and RSS conditions in an analogous lemma.

Lemma E.1
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, if
T\gtrsim M_{1}^{2}Dr_{\textup{total}}
, then with probability at least
1-C\exp\{-CDr_{\textup{total}}\}
, for any matrix
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{V}^{L}_{\textup{unc}}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)
and the true value
\mathbf{A}^{*}\in\mathcal{V}^{L}_{\textup{unc}}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)
, we have

\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=L}^{T+L-1}\left\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*})\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

(E.73)

where
\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}
,
\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}
, and
M_{1}
are identical to those defined in Lemma
C.1
, and
r_{\textup{total}}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}r_{\ell}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.

The proof is perfectly analogous to the proof of Lemma
C.1
.
Denote
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{ND\times LND}
. Let
R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}):=T^{-1}\sum_{t=L}^{T+L-1}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. Let
\mathbf{X}_{\text{reg}}=[\mathbf{x}_{L},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{T+L-1}]\in\mathbb{R}^{(LND)\times T}
be the stacked regressor matrix (analogous to
\mathbf{Z}
in the lag-1 proof).
Let
\boldsymbol{\delta}=\text{vec}(\mathbf{\Delta})\in\mathbb{R}^{LN^{2}D^{2}\times 1}
,
\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}}=\mathbf{X}_{\text{reg}}\mathbf{X}_{\text{reg}}^{\top}/T\in\mathbb{R}^{(LND)\times(LND)}
, and
\mathbf{\Gamma}=\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}})
.
The quadratic form can be written as:

R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})=\text{Tr}(\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{X}_{\text{reg}}\mathbf{X}_{\text{reg}}^{\top}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top})/T=\text{vec}(\mathbf{\Delta})^{\top}((\mathbf{X}_{\text{reg}}\mathbf{X}_{\text{reg}}^{\top}/T)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{ND})\text{vec}(\mathbf{\Delta})=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Gamma}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{ND})\boldsymbol{\delta}.

(E.74)

The proof strategy is identical to that of Lemma
C.1
:

(i)

Bound the expectation
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}(\mathbf{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{ND})\boldsymbol{\delta}
from below using
\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. This part is unchanged as it depends on the stability of the stacked process
\mathbf{x}_{t}
(guaranteed by Assumptions
1
and
2
), not the parameter space complexity.

(ii)

Bound the concentration term
\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
using a covering number argument.

The only essential change is in step (ii). The parameter space
\mathcal{S}(r,D;N)
from the lag-1 proof is replaced by the lag-
L
parameter space
\mathcal{S}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)
.

Since the parameters
(\mathbf{U}_{\ell},\mathbf{V}_{\ell},\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell})
governing each block, are distinct and unconstrained across lags, the total degrees of freedom for the difference of the concatenated matrix
\mathbf{A}
is simply the sum of the intrinsic dimensions of individual blocks. Specifically, for the
\ell
-th block with rank
r_{\ell}
, the degrees of freedom contribute
4Dr_{\ell}+4
to the total dimension. Summing over all
L
blocks, the intrinsic dimension of the concatenated difference set
\mathcal{S}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)
is bounded by
\text{df}_{\textup{total}}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(4Dr_{\ell}+4)=4Dr_{\textup{total}}+4L
. Applying the same volumetric argument as in the single-block case, we obtain the covering number bound:

|\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)|\leq\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{4Dr_{\textup{total}}+4L}.

(E.75)

By replacing the complexity exponent
4Dr+4
in the union bound step of the lag-1 proof with this correct total degrees of freedom
\mathrm{df_{\textup{total}}}
, the rest of the proof follows identically.

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}_{L}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq 2\pi\tau\mathcal{M}(f_{\mathbf{Z}})\eta\right)\\
\leq&\dots\\
\leq&2\exp\left[-cT\min(\eta,\eta^{2})+(\mathrm{df_{TNLR}})\log(C/\varepsilon)\right].\end{split}

(E.76)

The same choice of
\eta
yields the desired high-probability bound. The constants
\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}
and
\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}
remain the same, as they depend on the spectral properties of
\mathbf{\Gamma}
(the covariance of the process), not the covering number of the parameter space.
∎

E.3.3
Property of deviation bound for RRNAR(
L
)

We now generalize the deviation bound from the lag-1 model (Section
C.4
) to the lag-
L
case.

Lemma E.2
.

Define

\xi_{L}:=\sup_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{V}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N)}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\ \mathbf{A}\right\rangle,

(E.77)

where
\mathcal{V}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},D;N):=\{\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{A}_{L}]\ |\ \mathbf{A}_{\ell}=(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{\top})\otimes(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}\mathbf{I}_{N}+\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}\mathbf{W}_{N}),\mathbf{U}_{\ell},\mathbf{V}_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r_{\ell}},\ \beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}\in\mathbb{R},\forall\ell,\left\|\mathbf{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\|\mathbf{A}_{\ell}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=1\}.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, if
T\gtrsim M_{1}^{2}Dr_{\textup{total}}
, then with probability as least
1-C\exp\left(-CDr_{\textup{total}}\right)
,

\xi_{L}\lesssim M_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}}{T}},

(E.78)

where
\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(2+r_{\ell}+2Dr_{\ell})
is the total degrees of freedom for the lag-
L
model, and
M_{1},M_{2}
are identical to those defined in Lemma
C.1
and
C.2
.

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.

The proof is perfectly analogous to the proof of Lemma
C.2
. The core of the proof relies on a covering number argument to bound the supremum of the parameter space
\mathcal{V}_{L}
.

The key steps are identical:

(i)

The deviation
\xi_{L}
is related to the supremum over an
\varepsilon
-covering net
\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},N;D)
and a remainder term. The gradient
\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})=(\sum_{t=L+1}^{T+L}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{x}_{t-1}^{\top})/T
replaces the lag-1 gradient.

(ii)

The concentration inequality for
S_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})=\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\overline{\mathbf{A}}\rangle
is applied.

(iii)

A union bound is taken over all elements
\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},N;D)
.

The only essential change is to replace the covering number exponent of the lag-1 space,
\mathrm{df}^{(1)}=\mathrm{df_{NLR}}
, with the total degrees of freedom of the lag-
L
space,
\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(2+r_{\ell}+2Dr_{\ell})
, which is derived as follows:

The new set
\mathcal{V}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},N;D)
contains concatenated matrices
\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{A}_{L}]
where
\left\|\mathbf{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1
and each block
\mathbf{A}_{\ell}
has the required structure
\mathbf{A}_{\ell}=\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1\ell}
.
The complexity for each component is determined by its intrinsic parameterization:

•

\mathrm{df}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell})=2
. This space is defined by the two parameters
(\beta_{A,\ell},\beta_{N,\ell})
. This parameter count is consistent with the intrinsic dimension derived in the analysis of
\mathcal{R}_{1}(d)
(Lemma
C.5
).

•

\mathrm{df}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell})=r_{\ell}+2Dr_{\ell}
. A rank-
r_{\ell}
matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
(parameterized by
\mathbf{U}_{\ell},\mathbf{V}_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times r_{\ell}}
) is determined by its
r_{\ell}
singular values and its
D\times r_{\ell}
left and right singular vectors. This count is consistent with the intrinsic dimension derived in the analysis of
\mathcal{R}(d,r)
(Lemma
C.4
).

The total parameter space for all
L
lags is the union of these independent spaces, so its total degrees of freedom is the sum of the individual complexities:

|\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},N;D)|\leq\prod_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\frac{C_{\ell}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\mathrm{df}^{(\ell)}}\leq\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathrm{df}^{(\ell)}}=\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\mathrm{df}_{\textup{TNLR}}},

(E.79)

where

\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\mathrm{df}(\mathbf{B}_{1\ell})+\mathrm{df}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell})\right)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(2+r_{\ell}+2Dr_{\ell}).

(E.80)

The union bound step thus becomes:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\xi_{L}\geq(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)x\right)\\
\leq&\sum_{\overline{\mathbf{A}}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},N;D)}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\geq(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)x\right)\\
\leq&|\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{L}(\boldsymbol{r},N;D)|\left(\exp\left\{-\frac{(1-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)^{2}Tx^{2}\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})}{3\sigma_{\max}^{2}(\Sigma_{e})}\right\}+C\exp\{-c\cdot\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}\}\right)\\
\leq&\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}}\left(\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{1}Tx^{2}}{M_{2}^{2}}\right\}+C\exp\{-c\cdot\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}\}\right).\end{split}

(E.81)

Setting
x=C_{2}M_{2}\sqrt{\mathrm{df_{TNLR}}/T}
for a sufficiently large constant
C_{2}
ensures that the right-hand side is bounded by
C^{\prime}\exp(-C^{\prime}\cdot\mathrm{df_{TNLR}})
, completing the proof.
∎

E.3.4
Property of initial value for RRNAR(
L
)

The scaled gradient descent algorithm
E.1
requires a high-quality initial estimator
\mathbf{\Theta}^{(0)}=\{(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell}^{(0)},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}^{(0)},\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(0)},\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(0)})\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
that satisfies the conditions specified in Corollary
E.1
.

This estimator is obtained by adapting the procedure from Section
C.5
to the lag-
L
case. We solve a structured optimization problem for all
L
lags simultaneously:

(\{\widehat{\beta}_{\textup{A},\ell}\},\{\widehat{\beta}_{N,\ell}\},\{\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2\ell}\}):=\arg\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell}\in\mathbb{R}\\
\text{rank}(\mathbf{B}_{2\ell})\leq r_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta}),

(E.82)

where
\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mathbf{\Theta})
is the full lag-
L
loss function (
E.2
).

As this problem is non-convex, we compute an effective solution using an Alternating Least Squares (ALS) approach, analogous to the lag-1 case:

•

Solving for all
(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell})
:
Given the set of
\{\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
, the problem becomes a single, large Ordinary Least Squares (OLS) problem for all
2L
network parameters.

•

Solving for all
\mathbf{B}_{2\ell}
:
Given the set of
\{(\beta_{\textup{A},\ell},\beta_{\textup{N},\ell})\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
, the problem conveniently decouples into
L
independent Reduced-Rank Regression (RRR) problems. For each lag
\ell
, we solve for
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{2\ell}
using the standard RRR solution.

We run a small number of these alternating steps to obtain
(\{\tilde{\beta}_{\textup{A},\ell}\},\{\tilde{\beta}_{\textup{N},\ell}\},\{\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2\ell}\})
. Finally, we compute the rank-
r_{\ell}
SVD of each
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2\ell}
to obtain the initial factors
(\mathbf{U}_{\ell}^{(0)},\mathbf{V}_{\ell}^{(0)})
. This practical procedure provides an
\mathbf{A}^{(0)}
that satisfies the initialization conditions required by Corollary
E.1
.

Appendix F
Additional Explanations of Simulation Results

In this section, we provide the specific details of Figures
1
and
2
in the Section
5.2
of the main article.

Figure
1
in the main article presents the simulation results averaged over 100 replications. To visualize asymptotic behaviors across the network size
N
, all panels are plotted on log-log scales. To balance detail with clarity, we employ a consistent graphical strategy across the figure. Bold solid lines depict the central tendency, smoothed using an 8-window Moving Average to filter stochastic volatility, while the surrounding faint shaded regions represent the Interquartile Range (IQR) to illustrate sampling variability. Blue and red hues denote sparse (
k=3
) and dense (
k=\lfloor N/2\rfloor
) network topologies, respectively.

Panels (a) and (b) verify the property of independent of
N
. Panel (a) displays the
overall global matrix error, which fluctuates around the global mean, indicated by the dashed
horizontal line, without a discernible trend. In Panel (b), given the structural similarity of
the low-rank factors, we aggregate the statistics of
\mathbf{U}
and
\mathbf{V}
to compute the background IQR and the smoothed trend line. However, we retain distinct markers, circles for
\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{U}}}
and triangles for
\mathcal{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}}
, to visually each’s original fluctuation. The results confirm that the projection errors remain robust to variations in
N
.

Panels (c) and (d) validate the asymptotic convergence rates derived in Corollary
1
. A
grey dotted line with a slope of
-1
serves as the theoretical reference for
\mathcal{O}(N^{-1})
decay. Panel (c) demonstrates that the error for the autoregressive parameter
\beta_{\textup{A}}
decays linearly along this reference line for both sparse and dense networks, confirming the
\mathcal{O}((NT)^{-1})
rate. In contrast, Panel (d) reveals a critical topological dichotomy. The error for the network parameter
\beta_{\textup{N}}
exhibits the blessing of dimensionality (tracking the
-1
slope) only in the sparse setting. In the dense setting, the error remains asymptotically flat. This perfectly
corroborates our theory, as the normalizing factor
\|\mathbf{W}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
grows linearly with
N
in sparse graphs but remains constant in dense graphs.

Figure
2
in the main article presents the simulation results averaged over 100 replications. To visualize the trends clearly, we employ a consistent encoding: dark solid lines with markers depict the empirical mean error, serving as the primary performance indicator; faint dashed lines represent linear regression fits to test the theoretical growth rate; and translucent shaded bands show the stability (mean ±1 standard deviation).

The results demonstrate a robust linear dependency on
D
across all metrics, corroborating the
\mathcal{O}(Dr)
complexity derived in Corollary
1
. As shown in Panels (a) and (b), the overall
error and the subspace projection errors exhibit a near-perfect linear relationship with
D
(
R^{2}\approx 0.99
). The narrow shaded bands in these panels indicate that the estimation of
high-dimensional components is highly deterministic and stable. Similarly, Panels (c) and (d) reveal that the errors for the scalar parameters
\beta_{\textup{A}}
and
\beta_{\textup{N}}
also scale linearly with
D
.

Appendix G
Additional Real Data Example Details

G.1
Network Topology of PeMS08

To elucidate the underlying spatial dependency patterns within the PeMS08 dataset, we visualize the sensor network topology in Figure
G.1
. The graph consists of
N=170
sensors (nodes). The topology is visualized using a force-directed layout algorithm. Node size is proportional to the weighted degree centrality, highlighting key traffic hubs. Edge thickness and color intensity represent the strength of the spatial weights, with thicker, darker lines indicating stronger spatial correlations and closer proximity.

Figure G.1
:
Visualization of the spatial dependency structure in the PeMS08 traffic network.

The visualization uncovers distinct structural heterogeneity in the traffic system. First, the significantly larger nodes, characterized by high weighted degree centrality, serve as the hubs of the network. In the context of highway systems, these nodes likely correspond to major interchanges or segments with high sensor density. They act as information aggregators in the graph convolution operation, playing a pivotal role in diffusing traffic states across different branches of the network. Second, the prominent spine formed by thick, dark-blue edges illustrate the backbone of the highway system. These edges represent strong spatial weights, indicating continuous road segments where traffic flow dynamics (e.g., shockwave propagation) are highly correlated and immediate.

G.2
Network Topology of SMD

Figure
G.2
illustrates the network topology of the Server Machine Dataset, comprising 28 nodes arranged in a ring configuration to uniformly display the interaction structure within the server cluster. The network connectivity is established based on the Pearson correlation of the CPU utilization series, effectively capturing the synchronization of workload fluctuations between machines.

In this visualization, the size of each node is proportional to its degree centrality, highlighting servers that act as connectivity hubs within the network. Correspondingly, the thickness of the edges reflects the strength of these statistical dependencies; thicker connections denote pairs of servers with highly coupled operational dynamics, revealing underlying load-balancing mechanisms or shared computational tasks within the data center.

Figure G.2
:
Visualization of the spatial dependency structure in the SMD network.

G.3
Detailed Feature Description for SMD

Table
G.1
provides a detailed description of the 38 features in the Server Machine Dataset (SMD) used in Section
6.2
. The features are categorized into CPU metrics, memory usage, disk I/O, and network traffic.

Table G.1
:
Description of the 38 Features in the SMD.

Feature Name

Description

Category: CPU Metrics

CPU Load

Average system load (process queue length) over the last minute.

CPU_Sys

Percentage of CPU time spent in kernel space (System mode).

CPU Usr

Percentage of CPU time spent in user space (User mode).

CPU_Nice

Percentage of CPU time spent on low-priority user processes.

CPU_IO

Percentage of CPU time waiting for I/O operations.

CPU_Irq

Percentage of CPU time serving hardware interrupts.

CPU Sft

Percentage of CPU time serving software interrupts.

CPU Stl

Percentage of CPU time stolen by the hypervisor (Steal time).

CPU_Gst

Percentage of CPU time running a virtual CPU (Guest time).

CPU_Gni

Percentage of CPU time running a niced guest.

CPU Idl

Percentage of CPU time spent idle.

CPU_Tot

Total CPU utilization (100% minus Idle time).

Category: Memory Usage

Mem Use

Amount of physical memory currently in use.

Mem Free

Amount of physical memory currently free.

Mem Buf

Amount of memory used as buffers (e.g., for block devices).

Mem Cac

Amount of memory used as cache (e.g., page cache).

Mem_SwpT

Total amount of swap space available.

Mem_SwpU

Amount of swap space currently in use.

Mem_SwpF

Amount of swap space currently free.

Mem Act

Amount of active memory (recently used, not reclaimable).

Mem Ina

Amount of inactive memory (candidate for reclamation).

Mem Pag

Amount of memory used for page tables.

Category: Disk I/O

Dsk Rd

Disk read throughput (data read per second).

Dsk Wr

Disk write throughput (data written per second).

Dsk Rt

Average time required to service a read request.

Dsk Wt

Average time required to service a write request.

Dsk Q

Average length of the disk queue (requests waiting for service).

Dsk Utl

Percentage of time the disk was busy (Disk utilization).

Dsk Svc

Average service time per I/O request.

Dsk Wait

Average total wait time per I/O request (queue + service).

Category: Network Traffic

Net Recv

Network receiving rate (bytes received per second).

Net Send

Network sending rate (bytes sent per second).

Net PktI

Number of packets received per second.

Net PktO

Number of packets sent per second.

Net ErrI

Number of errors detected in received packets.

Net ErrO

Number of errors detected in sent packets.

Net_DrpI

Number of received packets dropped (e.g., buffer full).

Net_DrpO

Number of sent packets dropped.
